  - [x] Bootstrap blocking monotonic deadline timer with `time.sleepUntil(deadlineMillis)`: the absolute `i64` deadline composes directly with `time.monotonic()`, already-expired deadlines return immediately, native lowering rechecks the monotonic clock around EINTR-safe sleeps to avoid cumulative relative-sleep drift, LSP completion/signature help expose the contract, and unreachable timer support tree-shakes completely. Asynchronous task-integrated timers remain pending with the task/structured-concurrency model.
- [x] Bounded channel/message passing for the current Copy-scalar bootstrap: `channel.open(capacity) -> (i64, error)` creates a compiler-owned bounded FIFO, `channel.write(handle, value) -> error` and `channel.read(handle) -> (i64, error)` transfer `i64` values with native blocking backpressure, and `channel.close(handle) -> error` wakes blocked operations and deterministically releases channel storage once in-flight operations leave. Capacity is bounded to `1..=65536`, invalid/closed handles fail explicitly, the pthread runtime tree-shakes when unreachable, and LSP completion/signature help expose the API. Richer payload types remain naturally sequenced behind their ownership/sendability rules rather than requiring source generics.
- [x] Worker/thread execution for the current capture-free bootstrap: `worker.start(work: fn() -> void) -> (i64, error)` launches compiler-owned work on a native pthread, `worker.startWith(work: fn(i64) -> void, argument: i64) -> (i64, error)` passes one Copy scalar argument without capture, and `worker.join(handle) -> error` waits exactly once through an opaque handle registry with explicit invalid/unknown/already-joined failures. The worker entry contract cannot capture outer locals, so borrowed application storage is not smuggled across the thread boundary; native pthread/runtime support tree-shakes when unreachable and LSP completion/signature help expose the API. Task results, structured concurrency, and broader sendability remain tracked by their dedicated concurrency items rather than being implied by this primitive; cooperative cancellation propagation is completed separately above.
- [ ] Data-race prevention integrated with ownership. Detached async parameters/results already require structural `Send`; continuation lowering now applies the same boundary to every compiler-saved local/pattern value that could resume on a child completion thread, while non-Send `Copy` locals that genuinely cross suspension stay on their originating task thread through blocking await lowering. Non-Send locals declared only after the function's final suspension remain task-local and no longer disable safe continuation lowering. General owned resources, exclusive mutable borrows, and broader source-visible thread-transfer contracts remain required before this item is complete.
- [x] Event-loop integration for UI targets: Linux applications enter the native GTK application loop and route control callbacks through GTK signals, while Android applications enter through `ANativeActivity_onCreate` and route native control/lifecycle callbacks through compiler-generated JNI entry points. Cross-target code-generation regression coverage pins both event-loop roots and callback dispatch paths without introducing a Flux-side event-loop or bridge layer.
- [ ] Server-oriented high-throughput I/O runtime. Bootstrap server networking now includes cancellation-aware finite/immediate/infinite TCP accept plus TCP/UDP receive waits and bounded scatter/gather TCP backpressure through `net.writePartsTimeout`, which preserves borrowed `str[]` fragments in native `sendmsg`/`iovec` form under one monotonic deadline and returns exact completed-byte progress on timeout/failure/cancellation. Resumable scatter/gather TCP output through `net.writePartsFrom(socket, parts, offset)` uses one caller-owned global byte cursor across borrowed fragments, makes one nonblocking `sendmsg` progress attempt, treats `EAGAIN`/`EWOULDBLOCK` as a clean zero-progress retry point, and avoids concatenation or retained payload state; the helper tree-shakes when unreachable and participates in LSP completion/signature help. Resumable single-text and scatter/gather sends reject statically known literal cursors beyond statically known literal payload lengths while retaining native bounds checks for dynamic cursors. Resumable timed backpressure through `net.writeFromTimeout(socket, text, offset, timeoutMillis)` and `net.writePartsFromTimeout(socket, parts, offset, timeoutMillis)` combines those caller-owned byte cursors with one monotonic total deadline and returns `(nextOffset, complete, error)`, preserving exact forward progress across timeout, cancellation, and native send failures without retaining borrowed payload state. Both APIs require connected nonblocking TCP sockets, use cancellation-aware readiness waits after `EAGAIN`/`EWOULDBLOCK`, reject invalid literal offsets/timeouts statically where possible with runtime guards for dynamic values, tree-shake when unreachable, and participate in LSP completion/signature help. A task-integrated event loop, queued backpressure policy, and binary buffers remain required before this item is complete.

Verified bounded concurrent-server scheduling follow-up: `http.serveConcurrent` now reaps completed request workers continuously instead of retaining them until a 64-request batch barrier. When all 64 tracked slots are occupied, compiler-owned worker completion/cancellation signalling wakes the server as soon as any tracked request finishes rather than blocking on the oldest handle, after which every completed request is reaped before accepting more work. This preserves bounded structured ownership while removing the remaining oldest-request head-of-line stall at capacity. The completion wait wakes promptly on structured cancellation as well. This advances the unchecked structured-concurrency, server-oriented high-throughput I/O, and Milestone 28 efficient-I/O tracks without claiming those broader items complete.

Verified timed batched socket follow-up: `net.acceptManyTimeout(listener, maxCount, timeoutMillis, callback)` performs one cancellation-aware initial listener-readiness wait before the existing caller-bounded nonblocking TCP accept drain and returns exact accepted-count plus readiness/error without retaining accepted sockets; `net.readManyTimeout(socket, maxBytes, maxCount, timeoutMillis, callback)` and `net.readManyFromTimeout(socket, maxBytes, maxCount, timeoutMillis, callback)` apply the same model to TCP/UDP receive drains. These APIs preserve partial drain progress on cancellation/failure, validate batch/timeout bounds statically where possible and at runtime, tree-shake when unreachable, and participate in LSP completion/signature help. The broader task-integrated event-loop, queued-backpressure, and binary-buffer work remains unchecked.

Verified caller-bounded concurrent HTTP admission follow-up: `http.serveConcurrentLimit(listener, maxHeadBytes, maxBodyBytes, maxConcurrent, requestCallback, headerCallback, bodyCallback)` preserves structured request-worker ownership while allowing a caller-selected `1..=64` in-flight cap. Constant limits are checked statically, dynamic limits are guarded at runtime, and a full server waits on compiler-owned worker-completion signalling then reaps completed requests before accepting more. The default `http.serveConcurrent` remains capped at 64. This supplies request-level backpressure and advances the unchecked structured-concurrency, server-oriented high-throughput I/O, and Milestone 28 efficient-I/O tracks without claiming the remaining task event-loop, general queued socket-backpressure, binary-buffer, or ownership work complete.

Verified concurrent-server error-drain follow-up: `http.serveConcurrent` and `http.serveConcurrentLimit` now cancel and join only their tracked request workers before returning on parser/child failures, structured cancellation, listener/poll/accept failures, and request-state/start failures. The triggering error is preserved instead of leaving hidden in-flight request work for later `worker.joinAll()` cleanup. `serveConcurrentLimit` also independently pulls in the cancellable HTTP parser and poll runtime when used without `serveConcurrent`. Native regression coverage keeps one request in a cancellation-aware sleep, fails a sibling request with malformed framing, then proves handled server failure leaves no owned request workers for a subsequent `worker.joinAll()`. Structured concurrency and server-oriented high-throughput I/O remain unchecked because source-visible task-handle cancellation/failure aggregation, broader ownership/sendability, a task-integrated event loop, general queued backpressure, and binary buffers are still pending.

Verified concurrent-server queued-admission follow-up: once listener readiness is established, `http.serveConcurrent` and `http.serveConcurrentLimit` now drain already-queued TCP connections into every currently free request-worker slot before returning to the outer readiness loop. The drain performs zero-time readiness checks between accepts, never exceeds the caller/default concurrency cap, remains cancellation-aware, and cancels/joins already-owned request workers on listener/poll/start failures. This removes avoidable per-connection outer-loop polling while preserving bounded structured ownership. Structured concurrency and server-oriented high-throughput I/O remain unchecked because source-visible task-handle cancellation/failure aggregation, broader ownership/sendability, a task-integrated event loop, general queued backpressure, and binary buffers are still pending.

Verified bounded concurrent-request-state follow-up: `http.serveConcurrent` and `http.serveConcurrentLimit` now keep both request payload records and their worker-registry nodes in compiler-owned fixed 64-slot stack pools rather than allocating/freeing per-request payload or worker state. Active worker handles retain their exact slot identity; reaping joins completed workers before releasing those slots for reuse, heap-backed ordinary `worker.start*` handles keep their existing lifetime, and every server return path still drains its active workers before the stack-owned pools expire. Native `maxConcurrent = 1` coverage queues two requests and proves the same bounded slots can be safely reused for the second request. Structured concurrency and server-oriented high-throughput I/O remain unchecked because source-visible task-handle cancellation/failure aggregation, broader ownership/sendability, a task-integrated event loop, general queued backpressure, and binary buffers are still pending.

Verified structured worker-completion follow-up: `worker.done(handle) -> (bool, error)` provides a non-consuming scope-confined completion check, while `worker.waitAny(handles: i64[]) -> (i64, error)` blocks on compiler-owned completion signalling until one direct child finishes without joining or consuming any handle. `waitAny` validates every borrowed handle before waiting, preserves structured-scope ownership, wakes promptly when workers finish, and cooperates with worker cancellation; callers still use ordinary `worker.join` to consume completed handles and surface descendant failures. Native regression coverage verifies completion ordering, empty/input-type failures, and dead-code tree shaking, while LSP completion/signature help expose both calls. This gives bounded worker pools the same first-completion primitive already used internally by concurrent HTTP serving, advancing the unchecked structured-concurrency and server-oriented high-throughput tracks without claiming the remaining async task-handle/failure aggregation, ownership/sendability, event-loop, queued-backpressure, or binary-buffer work complete.

Verified structured join-any follow-up: `worker.joinAny(handles: i64[]) -> (i64, error)` waits for the first completed direct child and consumes that child in the same source-level operation, returning the completed handle together with any descendant structured-scope failure surfaced by the join. It reuses the compiler-owned completion condition rather than polling, retains scope confinement and cancellation-aware waiting, rejects empty or mistyped handle collections, tree-shakes when unreachable, and is exposed through LSP completion/signature help. This reduces bounded worker-pool bookkeeping while advancing structured concurrency and server-oriented high-throughput scheduling without claiming source-visible async task handles, general event-loop integration, broader ownership/sendability, queued backpressure, or binary-buffer work complete.

# Milestone 7 — Typed Flux IR and optimizing native backend

Performance acceptance for this milestone is semantic as well as benchmarked: abstractions that can disappear must disappear, required safety checks must remain correct, and representative optimized Flux programs must be continuously compared against equivalent optimized Rust/C baselines so regressions are visible before release.

- [x] Typed Flux IR with source spans: every current expression shape is represented by deterministic typed values with source spans/dependencies, and control-dependent nested evaluation now has explicit source-spanned IR regions for short-circuit RHS, branches, match guards/arms, comprehensions, and deferred anonymous-function bodies rather than being recoverable only by re-reading checked AST shape.
  - [x] Bootstrap per-function semantic CFG nodes retain original source spans, function parameter/return types, and explicit typed local-binding metadata while expressions remain in the checked AST.
  - [x] Every normalized statement-level expression evaluation now carries its exact canonical static value shape in CFG/IR, including multi-value calls, loop/match guards, pattern-scoped expressions, redirects, and destructuring sources.
  - [x] All currently supported nested expression shapes lower into deterministic typed IR values with dependency IDs, including literals/names, unary/binary operations, calls/pipelines, lists/spreads/conditional items, indexing/slicing, struct construction/fields, conditionals, value-producing match/list-match guards and arms, and comprehension iterable/filter/body expressions. Control-dependent subexpressions now also attach to explicit source-spanned IR regions that carry their governing condition/match/loop/deferred semantics for later backend consumption.
  - [x] Typed value dependencies now classify eager, short-circuit, branch, match, loop, and deferred-body uses and derive per-value reachability from reachable CFG evaluation roots. Source-foldable short-circuit and conditional-list branches prune unselected nested values immediately, while cross-statement propagated constants remain distinct so symbol elimination cannot outrun the bootstrap AST backend before direct typed-IR lowering lands.
  - [x] Verified backend-consumption follow-up: pure propagated boolean short-circuit values (`&&`/`||`) now lower from typed-IR constants at binding, assignment, and return boundaries; effectful, dynamic, ambiguous, and ownership-sensitive expressions retain the checked-AST path.
  - [x] Verified scalar-string backend-consumption follow-up: propagated static `str` values now lower from typed-IR constants at the same boundaries; runtime borrowed strings and ownership-sensitive values retain ordinary lowering.
  - Verified effect-boundary follow-up: normalized reachable typed-IR graphs now expose deterministic direct named callees and intrinsic suspension/opaque/interface-dispatch effects, while deferred anonymous bodies remain separately queryable. The semantic database aggregates these as per-function summaries for future whole-program solving. Compile-time-dead branches are excluded from the call set; this provides the graph boundary for future explicit-effects checking without claiming source-level effect annotations complete.
- [ ] Ownership facts represented in IR.
  - [x] Bootstrap CFG action/evaluation nodes carry deterministic binding-read sets plus explicit source/destination/span facts for direct non-copy local moves.
  - [x] Direct-local move path-state and reachability are computed to a fixed point on CFG edges and exposed per node; bootstrap use-after-move checking consumes these IR facts.
  - [x] Typed IR values carry a normalized `Copy` versus immutable-borrow ownership class derived from the canonical type system, with a CFG query and regression coverage; owned-value classes remain gated on owned transfer/drop semantics.
  - [ ] Add borrow regions, partial moves, drops, lifetimes, and consuming-call ownership facts to normalized CFG/IR analysis. Bootstrap immutable borrow regions/lifetimes are now explicit as path- and liveness-aware per-node borrow states derived from reaching definitions, with deterministic CFG-edge end facts marking the exact branch/path where each live borrow ceases; live-borrow move validation consumes those normalized IR facts directly instead of re-walking borrow provenance at each move. Normalized drop boundaries and consuming-call modes are represented and queryable from the CFG; partial moves and actual owned-value consuming semantics remain pending.
    - [x] Verified node-local drop projection follow-up: definition-scoped release facts computed from normalized definition liveness are attached directly to their CFG boundary node through `ControlFlowOwnership.drops` and exposed by `drops_at`, while the deterministic graph-level index remains available. This gives future owned-resource lowering a node-local ownership event stream without claiming that bootstrap list views own storage or that destructors/partial moves/consuming calls exist.
    - [x] Typed call boundaries now remain explicit ownership facts on each normalized evaluation node, retaining the callee identity, source span, and typed argument value IDs for ordinary, qualified, optional-cascade, and interface-dispatch calls. Argument provenance also recursively retains the exact reaching source definitions through field, index, slice, and optional collection projections, so future consuming-call analysis can query the same typed value graph without rebuilding AST walks or losing the owner behind a projection. Non-copy collection arguments additionally expose their borrowed owner definitions and `ImmutableBorrow` argument mode separately from ordinary `Copy` arguments, with alias types canonicalized before classification, keeping future immutable-lifetime and consuming-call checks on one typed boundary. Consuming `drop` move facts now retain the same typed source value ID as their call fact, so the complete ownership event stream cannot lose the value boundary at the release site. This establishes the value-based boundary needed for future consuming-call analysis without inventing a source consuming-call form; partial moves, drops, and actual consuming semantics remain pending.
    - [x] Verified nested call-argument provenance follow-up: normalized ownership facts now retain list owners hidden inside nested list literals, reachable list conditionals/matches, projections, and compiler-known list transforms, while compile-time-unselected list branches are pruned from the definition set. Regression coverage verifies both owner retention and dead-branch exclusion without introducing a consuming-call syntax or claiming owned-value transfer complete.
    - [x] Verified explicit drop boundaries: `drop` over a bootstrap list, set, map, or ownership-tracked optional list is represented as a typed consuming call with no borrowed-argument provenance, and its exact direct source definition is also attached as a normalized move event for use-after-move checking. Native collection descriptors remain allocation-free until owned storage and destructor semantics land.
    - [x] Verified drop projection safety: `drop` accepts only a named non-copy collection binding, so nested-list/index/property projections remain immutable borrows and cannot be mistaken for owned storage without a normalized consuming move event.
    - [x] Verified non-copy collection boundary follow-up: normalized ownership facts classify set/map descriptors, as well as lists and supported optional collection descriptors, as `ImmutableBorrow` at ordinary call and return boundaries and retain their reaching source definitions; `Copy` scalar/value boundaries remain distinct, and owned collection transfer remains pending.
    - [x] Verified exact move-event provenance: every normalized non-copy transfer and consuming `drop` event now carries the reaching source-definition identities used by CFG move analysis, preserving distinct shadowed/re-executed bindings instead of requiring ownership consumers to resolve names again. Partial moves and owned-resource semantics remain pending.
    - [x] Partial-move provenance groundwork: normalized non-copy transfers preserve an ordered typed named-aggregate field path alongside the reaching root definition and retain the exact node-local projected value when available, while list properties/projections remain classified as immutable borrows; this records the distinction needed for future partial-move validation without claiming aggregate ownership transfer or destructor semantics.
    - [x] Partial-move query boundary: normalized CFG consumers can enumerate whole-value versus projected move events, including node-local queries, through the typed ownership API; current bootstrap aggregate-field moves remain rejected before normalization, so this exposes provenance without claiming partial-transfer semantics.
    - [x] Consuming-call projection provenance follow-up: every normalized typed call now retains the ordered named-aggregate field path for each argument value, so future consuming-call and partial-move analysis can distinguish a whole-value argument from `aggregate.field` without reconstructing checked expressions; bootstrap calls remain non-consuming except for the existing explicit `drop` boundary.
    - [x] Verified whole-versus-partial move query follow-up: normalized move state now exposes separate definition-scoped predicates for complete-value consumption and partial projection consumption, while the existing overlap query continues to conservatively account for either form; regression coverage verifies that same-named definitions remain independent.
    - [x] Complete node ownership-event view: normalized CFG consumers can inspect borrows, moves, typed calls, return transfers, and definition-scoped drops through one borrowed, allocation-free per-node event stream with deterministic category ordering; the broader item remains open for actual owned-value transfer, partial-move execution, and resource destruction semantics.
    - [x] Consuming-argument query boundary: normalized ownership calls now expose one safe view combining a consuming argument's projected path, exact reaching source definitions, and typed value identity, so future owned-aggregate/resource consumers cannot accidentally correlate parallel vectors or reinterpret immutable borrows as transfers; actual owned-value transfer remains pending.
    - [x] Borrow value-identity follow-up: each normalized immutable-borrow event now retains the exact typed value ID that produced the reachable read, alongside its source span and reaching source definitions. Duplicate suppression is value-identity aware even when source spans coincide, so ownership consumers can correlate borrow provenance with the normalized value graph without reconstructing an AST read; native behavior is unchanged and regression coverage verifies the identity.
    - [x] Reachability-filtered ownership event follow-up: normalized CFG consumers can enumerate the complete per-node ownership event stream while excluding events attached to statically unreachable `if`/`match`/loop regions, without allocating a second event buffer. This keeps dead moves and borrows from entering later ownership validation; native behavior is unchanged and regression coverage verifies dead versus reachable consuming events.
    - [x] Reachability-filtered borrow-boundary follow-up: normalized lifetime consumers can enumerate immutable-borrow start/end edges while excluding boundaries whose CFG endpoints are unreachable, preserving the same allocation-free borrowed view used by ownership events. Current normalization removes statically dead branches before emitting borrow edges; regression coverage verifies that all emitted live list-view boundaries remain available through the filtered stream.
    - [x] Definition-identity liveness is now a separate normalized CFG fact from the legacy name-based dead-store liveness: borrow-state construction consumes exact reaching borrower definitions, so same-named sibling bindings cannot keep one another's immutable borrow regions live at branch-local reads. A regression exposes the before/after definition sets and verifies each branch retains only its own binding.
    - [x] Return-boundary ownership facts now retain each normalized returned value ID, exact reaching source definitions, and `Copy` versus immutable-borrow classification. The facts are queryable from the CFG and preserve the value span for future owned-result transfer checks; current type checking continues to reject escaping list results until owned storage and lifetime semantics exist.
    - [x] Verified borrow-boundary query follow-up: normalized immutable-borrow start/end edge facts are also exposed through one deterministic borrowed `ControlFlowBorrowBoundary` stream, with edge-filtered queries preserving exact borrower/source definitions and spans. Ownership consumers can now inspect complete lifetime boundaries without correlating separate vectors or rebuilding CFG liveness; owned transfers, partial moves, and resource destruction remain pending.
- [ ] Explicit control-flow graph.
  - [x] The semantic database exposes deterministic per-function structural CFGs for sequential statements, `if`, loops/back-edges, `break`, `continue`, `return`, match-arm dispatch, and `else return` success/error exits.
  - [x] Bootstrap statement-level expression evaluation is explicit in CFG nodes for initializers, assignments, destructuring, return values, shell/redirection values, conditions, range bounds, iterables, match scrutinees, and match guards; `while`/`continue` edges re-enter condition evaluation correctly, while compile-time primitive conditions and statically empty integer ranges omit impossible branch/body edges.
  - [ ] Move expression evaluation/value production fully onto typed IR so later optimization no longer depends on checked-AST expression trees; all current expression shapes now have typed value/dependency nodes plus explicit control-dependent regions, while backend consumption of those IR values/regions remains pending.
    Verified expression-statement root follow-up: discarded expressions now use the same proof-aware typed-IR emission boundary as bindings, assignments, and returns, so proven-safe checked arithmetic lowers natively while operations that may still fail retain their runtime guards. General typed-IR expression emission remains pending.
    - [x] Verified pure expression-statement root consumption: when normalized typed IR proves a discarded scalar expression's complete value, code generation now consumes that root constant directly instead of rebuilding it from the checked AST; effectful, dynamic, ambiguous, and ownership-sensitive roots retain the safe fallback.
    - [x] Verified initial backend-consumption slice: reachable typed-IR name reads with one unambiguous propagated `Copy` constant now lower directly from the IR fact as a native literal at statement binding/assignment/return boundaries; ambiguous, dynamic, and ownership-sensitive values retain the existing safe lowering until complete typed-IR expression emission lands.
    - [x] Verified propagated-expression backend slice: uniquely identified reachable typed-IR scalar constants produced by pure arithmetic/comparison, unary, and scalar conditional values now lower directly as native literals at ordinary binding/assignment/return boundaries; boolean short-circuit regions, span collisions, dynamic values, calls, and ownership-sensitive expressions retain the safe AST path.
    - [x] Verified propagated-condition backend slice: normalized typed-IR boolean constants now feed ordinary `if` and `while` condition emission, including values hidden behind mutable local definitions that the legacy AST folder conservatively leaves dynamic; short-circuit, ambiguous, dynamic, and side-effecting conditions retain their existing safe lowering.
    - [x] Verified control-dependent purity follow-up: typed-IR scalar constant consumption treats values in pruned short-circuit/conditional regions as unevaluated, so a proven result such as `false && effect()` lowers to the IR constant without re-emitting the skipped call; reachable effectful values remain excluded from constant substitution.
    - [x] Verified additional scalar boundary consumption: normalized typed-IR constants now feed record/list/struct destructuring sources, optional-pattern conditions, shell values and redirect paths, integer range bounds, collection loop sources, and direct call/pipeline arguments at typed statement boundaries; aggregate, dynamic, effectful, and ownership-sensitive expressions continue through the checked-AST path.
    - [x] Verified nested scalar boundary consumption: reachable pure typed-IR constants are recursively substituted inside nested expression-statement calls and supported nested call/pipeline, collection, projection, conditional, match, and operator expressions before native emission, while preserving ordinary direct expression-statement bindings; span collisions, dynamic/effectful values, and ownership-sensitive expressions retain the checked-AST path.
    - [x] Verified async suspension boundary consumption: the normalized per-function scalar constant map is carried into continuation lowering, so straight-line and specialized await suspension calls substitute reachable pure typed-IR arguments before native task creation; dynamic, effectful, and ownership-sensitive await arguments retain the checked-AST path.
    - [x] Verified async match continuation consumption: enum/list async-match scrutinees and guards now use the same typed-IR constant/proof boundary when entering and resuming compiler-owned continuations, preserving the AST fallback for dynamic or ownership-sensitive values.
    - [x] Verified compound-argument recursion: typed-IR scalar constants are now substituted beneath list/set/map literals, list conditionals/comprehensions, records/structs, projections, and value-producing match expressions when those compounds are passed to ordinary, shell, or awaited calls; ownership-sensitive values still use the safe AST path.
    - [x] Verified multi-value boundary consumption: typed-IR scalar constants are now substituted through multi-value call arguments used by destructuring assignments/bindings and return forwarding, closing the remaining call-boundary gap while retaining the checked-AST fallback for dynamic, effectful, and ownership-sensitive values.
    - [x] Verified match-region consumption: enum/list/map match scrutinees, guards, map keys, and arm result expressions now consume reachable typed-IR scalar constants and checked-arithmetic proofs through native statement and value-producing match lowering, while payload-dependent and otherwise dynamic expressions retain the safe AST path.
    - [x] Verified short-circuit constant consumption: typed-IR propagation now proves `false && dynamic` and `true || dynamic` from the determining left operand without requiring an unrelated right-side constant; backend substitution still requires the complete value graph to be pure, so effectful right-hand expressions retain their observable short-circuit evaluation.
    - [x] Verified expression-root traversal follow-up: expression-statement roots now recursively visit unary/binary/conditional, collection, projection, struct/record, and match forms before native emission, so nested pure typed-IR constants are not lost merely because the statement has no expected result type; call, qualified-call, shell, and pipeline arguments now use the same exhaustive child rewrite, including list spread/optional wrappers. Dead pure roots remain removed by CFG DCE and effectful/dynamic/ownership-sensitive expressions retain their existing fallback.
    - [x] Verified anonymous-helper backend follow-up: capture-free anonymous function bodies, including compiler-fused `map`/`where`/`fold`/`reduce` callbacks, now receive a normalized synthetic CFG, so reachable pure scalar constants and checked-arithmetic proofs are consumed during native lambda/loop emission instead of silently falling back to checked-AST expression rebuilding; dynamic, effectful, and ownership-sensitive callback values retain the existing safe path.
    - Verified typed-value shape follow-up: normalized CFG values now distinguish set literals from list literals and retain record-literal field/value dependencies instead of collapsing records to `Opaque`; purity, value-use, collection-provenance, and dead-value traversals preserve those dependencies, with compiler regression coverage for both shapes. Native backend consumption of these aggregate IR nodes remains pending.
- [x] SSA or equivalent optimization-friendly value representation.
  - [x] Normalized evaluation sites produce deterministic typed value IDs for every single- or multi-value result, with producer node, result index, source span, canonical type, and common nested operand dependencies.
  - [x] CFG nodes now carry explicit typed local-definition facts for ordinary bindings/assignments, multi-value/list/struct destructuring, range/collection loop variables, and statement-match pattern bindings. Backward liveness consumes those normalized definitions rather than special-casing only mutable locals.
  - [x] Function parameters and normalized local definitions have deterministic definition identities, and every ordinary typed name-read records the exact may-reaching definition set computed over CFG edges. Branch and loop joins therefore expose explicit phi-equivalent input definitions without inventing runtime phi objects.
  - [x] Expression-local bindings also have typed scoped definition identities: value-producing enum/list-match payload patterns, comprehension variables, and anonymous-function parameters resolve their nested name reads to the exact scoped definition while preserving outer reaching-definition facts. Explicit conditional-expression control regions remain pending.
  - [x] Ordinary binding/assignment definition IDs link back to their producing typed IR values, so reaching-definition sets are usable as value-flow inputs rather than name-only facts.
- [x] Preserve debug locations through lowering: generated C emits source-aware `#line` directives for named/anonymous functions and every Flux statement, project builds retain canonical module paths, and Clang debug/profile DWARF line tables resolve native instructions back to the original `.flux` files and lines.
- [x] Constant folding for the current typed primitive/value-flow domain: compile-time expressions fold with checked Flux semantics and typed IR propagates primitive constants through local definition-use chains to prune impossible control flow before native lowering; aggregate/owned-value folding can extend the same mechanism as those value models land.
  - [x] Bootstrap primitive constant folding handles literals/constants, checked i64 arithmetic/comparisons, bool logic/short-circuiting, string equality, unary operations, and value-producing primitive conditionals before C lowering.
  - [x] Typed IR now propagates primitive constants to a fixed point through local definition-use chains, unary/binary operations, short-circuit boolean results, and conditional values. Constant local conditions prune impossible CFG edges and then recompute reaching definitions/liveness, enabling cross-statement dead-branch elimination without relying on C/LLVM to rediscover the fact.
- [x] Dead-code elimination.
  - [x] Bootstrap native lowering removes semantic-CFG-unreachable statements after terminating control flow (`return`, `break`, `continue`), literal or locally propagated compile-time-selected `if`/`while` branches, and statically empty integer ranges before emitting C. Typed value-root reachability plus local-definition liveness now cover the current bootstrap DCE surface; broader non-DCE CFG/SSA optimization remains tracked by the dedicated safety-check/abstraction-overhead criterion below.
  - [x] The CFG now computes deterministic backward live-before/live-after facts for parameters and every normalized local definition. Native lowering removes dead mutable-local assignments, omits dead mutable initializers when safe, erases dead immutable `Copy` bindings when their RHS is provably discardable, and now also drops the dead destination store for `Copy` bindings/assignments whose RHS must still execute: the optimized RHS is emitted only for its effects/traps, so calls, checked arithmetic, and checked indexing remain observable without materializing an unused local/store. It also prunes dead multi-value/list/struct destructuring extractions and statement-match pattern materialization, substitutes internal loop counters for dead source-level loop indexes, and avoids dead foreach element loads while retaining non-copy move semantics and list-pattern cardinality checks. Typed-IR value-root DCE now also removes reachable-but-semantically-dead pure expression roots and dead pure binding/assignment roots before whole-program reachability, recursively pruning their dependency values so dead function-value references no longer retain native symbols. Native lowering consumes those facts to omit dead non-escaping aggregate construction while preserving effectful/trapping work and non-copy moves.
  - [x] Follow-up: dead-store lowering recursively recognizes additional trap-free `Copy` RHS shapes instead of limiting elimination to primitive constants/name reads. Pure struct construction/update, struct-field projection, simple list literals with statically proven in-range indexing or non-trapping list properties, boolean/comparison expressions, and capture-free function-value construction can disappear when their result is dead, while dynamic list bounds, checked integer arithmetic/negation, calls, non-copy moves, and other potentially effectful or trapping work remain emitted. Compiler regression coverage pins both the newly erased forms and the retained safety/effect boundaries.
  Verified pure-equivalence DCE follow-up: dead same-value subtraction can now disappear when both equivalent operands are independently discardable, including identity-wrapped direct reads such as `(value + 0) - value`; potentially trapping checked subexpressions still remain observable rather than being erased merely because both sides are equivalent.
  Verified dead checked-negation DCE follow-up: dead unary negation and equivalent `0 - value` / `-1 * value` / `value / -1` forms now disappear completely when the operand is independently discardable and normalized range facts prove it cannot be `i64::MIN`. Proven-safe constant division therefore no longer survives merely to feed a dead negation, while unrestricted values and checked operands that can still overflow retain their authoritative runtime trap.
- [x] Inlining for the current bootstrap native backend: reachable private named functions lower as internal `static inline` native helpers, while `main` and explicit `pub` exports retain their external ABI. Clang can therefore inline private calls within the generated translation unit and release LTO can erase the call boundary entirely; address-taken private function values remain ordinary callable symbols when required. Generated-C regression coverage pins the linkage boundary, and a real release-build regression executes a private leaf helper then verifies it disappears from the optimized native symbol table.
  - [x] Bootstrap private named functions and capture-free anonymous functions lower with internal native linkage while `main` and explicit `pub` exports retain external linkage. This removes accidental interposition/ABI surface for compiler-owned internals and gives the current Clang/LTO backend full freedom to inline or internalize reachable private helpers without changing Flux source semantics; generated-C regression coverage pins the linkage boundary, and a real release-build regression executes a private leaf helper then verifies it disappears from the optimized native symbol table.
- [x] Escape analysis: normalized typed IR computes conservative transitive escape facts for reachable values crossing return, direct/qualified call, optional-cascade, and interface-dispatch boundaries, and follows name reads back through reaching definitions to their producing values. Native DCE consumes the same facts so dead non-escaping aggregate construction can disappear without treating non-copy moves as disposable; future owned resource kinds can extend the boundary/root classification without rebuilding source-AST escape logic.
- [x] Bounds-check elimination where proven safe for current list operations: compiler-generated iteration/transform/match/destructure accesses lower unchecked only behind compiler-established cardinality/range facts, and provably in-range literal `first`/`last`/`single` accesses skip redundant checks while dynamic/unproven indexes keep explicit checked semantics.
  - [x] Bootstrap compiler-generated list iteration, transform, reduction, sort/distinct/flatten/concat, spread-copy, comprehension, boolean aggregation, destructuring, and list-match extraction use unchecked strided element access only after compiler-emitted length guards establish the index range. Direct list-literal indexing plus `first`/`last`/`single` also use unchecked access when the compiler can prove the selected position exists; dynamic or otherwise unproven indexing and cardinality failures retain explicit checked semantics.
- [x] Devirtualization/interface specialization for the current closed-world interface model: concrete receivers dispatch directly, typed interface-pack facts shrink dynamic target sets, single-target interface values erase tags/switches entirely, and exported interface-valued boundaries stay conservatively open.
  - [x] Concrete-receiver interface calls lower directly to the mapped free function, and whole-program reachability follows that exact mapping rather than retaining every implementation. When no interface value is materialized, private interface runtime layout/dispatch metadata disappears entirely.
  - [x] Concrete interface packing is represented explicitly in typed IR with interface/target/value identities. Closed-world dynamic dispatch, interface layouts, tags, pack helpers, and mapped implementation reachability specialize to only the concrete targets reachable through those IR pack facts; public interface-valued function boundaries remain conservatively open.
  - [x] Single-target interface value sets devirtualize further: their native representation carries the concrete value directly with no tag/union, and dynamic capability helpers lower to a direct mapped call with no runtime switch. Multi-target sets retain checked tagged dispatch.
- [x] Target-aware optimization pipeline benchmarked against equivalent optimized Rust/C implementations for representative compute, collection, dispatch, and native-UI workloads; performance regressions become release blockers rather than accepted framework overhead.
  - [x] Native-UI performance coverage compares equivalent GTK4 grid/Text/Button interaction across optimized Flux, C, and Rust builds: release artifact/code footprint has a configurable best-native binary-size gate, while display-backed startup/render readiness and native Button interaction are timed through AT-SPI with configurable best-native runtime gates. Push/PR CI runs the timing path inside an isolated D-Bus/Xvfb display so UI performance regressions are continuously enforced rather than relying on a developer desktop.
- [x] Native object generation through an LLVM-class backend initially: the native build pipeline streams checked/generated Flux C directly to the installed Clang toolchain, which lowers it through LLVM and emits the final target object/executable with debug, profile, release, target-triple, sysroot, LTO, instrumentation, and static-link options preserved. This milestone intentionally does not claim direct Flux-IR lowering; removing bootstrap C as the semantic backend remains the separate follow-up below.
  - [x] `flux emit-llvm <file.flux|package-dir|flux.toml> [-o file.ll]` exposes the same checked/generated native program as textual LLVM IR through Clang, preserving compiler diagnostics and making the LLVM backend artifact inspectable; direct typed-Flux-IR semantic lowering remains a separate follow-up.
- [x] Prove and eliminate redundant safety checks (including bounds checks) and abstraction overhead through CFG/SSA analysis while preserving checks whose safety preconditions cannot be established statically. Current bootstrap lowering erases paired boolean negation (`!!expr`) in ordinary native and dynamic UI expressions while preserving exact-once evaluation of effectful boolean operands. Checked integer double negation (`-(-expr)`) now keeps only the first overflow guard: once the inner negation succeeds, the outer native negation is statically safe, preserving the `i64::MIN` trap and exact-once evaluation while removing a redundant runtime check. Boolean equality/inequality against compile-time `true`/`false` now lowers directly to the dynamic operand or one negation in ordinary native and dynamic UI expressions, including constants on either side, while effectful operands still evaluate exactly once. Complementary same-binding boolean comparisons now also prove `flag == !flag` / `!flag == flag` false and `flag != !flag` / `!flag != flag` true, while repeated negated reads prove `!flag == !flag` true and `!flag != !flag` false; these folds remain restricted to direct binding reads so effectful expressions keep every source evaluation. Matched constant inverse arithmetic now keeps the first checked operation but lowers the proven-safe outer inverse directly for `(expr + k) - k`, `(k + expr) - k`, `(expr - k) + k`, and `k + (expr - k)`, preserving inner overflow/effect semantics while removing the second redundant overflow guard in ordinary native and dynamic UI lowering. Matching non-zero constant division/multiplication pairs now also erase the redundant outer multiplication overflow guard for `(expr / k) * k` and `k * (expr / k)`: the inner checked/validated division remains authoritative, effectful dividends still evaluate exactly once, and the resulting quotient can be multiplied by the same divisor without exceeding the original `i64` magnitude. Typed CFG value identities now extend those proofs across statement boundaries: a name read must resolve to exactly one reaching definition whose producing typed value establishes the matching additive or division/multiplication precondition; partial constant cancellation reuses the same range proof, dynamic multiply-then-divide retains only the required zero-divisor guard, and branch joins or reassigned operands with ambiguous/different definitions keep the general checked lowering. Focused generated-C plus native execution coverage pins both optimized and conservative paths.
  Verified multiplication/division follow-up: `(expr * k) / k` and `(k * expr) / k` for the same compile-time non-zero `i64` constant now keep the checked inner multiplication but lower the outer division directly, because successful multiplication proves the matching divisor cannot trigger division-by-zero or `i64::MIN / -1`. Effectful multiplicands still evaluate exactly once, unmatched/dynamic divisors retain the general checked division path, and ordinary native plus dynamic UI lowering share the proof; broader CFG/SSA proof coverage remains pending.
  Verified signed additive-inverse follow-up: checked additive chains now recognize compile-time constants that are exact signed inverses, so `(expr + k) + -k`, `-k + (expr + k)`, and `(expr - k) - -k` keep the first checked operation but lower the proven-safe outer operation directly. Constant subtraction also recognizes `k - (k - expr)`. The proof refuses unrepresentable `i64::MIN` negation, preserves exact-once evaluation of effectful inner operands, keeps unmatched outer checks, and is shared by ordinary native and dynamic UI lowering; broader CFG/SSA proof coverage remains pending.
  Verified boolean-absorption follow-up: direct repeated bindings now collapse `flag && (flag || rhs)` and `flag || (flag && rhs)` to one binding read, including equivalent negated-binding forms. Symmetric right-nested `flag && (pure || flag)` / `flag || (pure && flag)` and left-nested `(flag || pure) && flag` / `(flag && pure) || flag` forms also collapse when the otherwise-extra nested operand is proven discardable as a compile-time boolean or direct/negated binding read; effectful nested operands remain intact. Ordinary native and dynamic UI lowering share the proof, so redundant reads disappear without dropping any source-observable evaluation; broader CFG/SSA proof coverage remains pending.
  Verified nested-complement follow-up: direct repeated bindings now prove `flag && (!flag && rhs)` false and `flag || (!flag || rhs)` true after exactly one outer binding evaluation, including equivalent negated-binding forms. Symmetric `flag && (pure && !flag)` / `flag || (pure || !flag)` folds are admitted only when the preceding nested operand is statically discardable; effectful operands remain emitted and execute with the original short-circuit order. Ordinary native and dynamic UI lowering share the proof, with native execution coverage pinning both optimized results and preserved effects; broader CFG/SSA proof coverage remains pending.
  Verified boolean-resolution follow-up: complementary nested terms now simplify `flag || (!flag && rhs)` to `flag || rhs` and `flag && (!flag || rhs)` to `flag && rhs`, including the equivalent form where the complementary term is on the nested right. The safe left-nested mirror is also reduced when the complementary term is evaluated first, so `(!flag || rhs) && flag` becomes `flag && rhs` and `(!flag && rhs) || flag` becomes `flag || rhs` without changing whether an effectful `rhs` is reached. The proof removes redundant binding reads/negations while preserving short-circuit reachability and exact-once residual evaluation; ordinary native and dynamic UI lowering share the optimization, and native execution coverage pins the effect boundary.
  Verified pure-boolean predicate reuse follow-up: structurally equivalent side-effect-free boolean predicates built from direct bindings, negation, short-circuit boolean operators, and checked-`i64` comparisons now share one authoritative evaluation across idempotence, complement, equality/inequality, absorption, and resolution forms. Composite predicates such as repeated `(value + 1 > limit)` therefore keep one checked addition instead of duplicating it, while calls/effectful lookalikes retain every source evaluation and short-circuit reachability. Ordinary native and dynamic UI lowering share the proof, with native execution and focused boolean regression coverage pinning optimized results and preserved effects.
  Verified mixed-negation follow-up: equivalent checked negation forms now share the same proof across unary `-expr`, `0 - expr`, multiplication by `-1`, and division by `-1`. Once the first semantic negation succeeds, a following equivalent negation lowers directly without a second `i64::MIN` guard in ordinary native and dynamic UI code, while the first guard remains authoritative. The same result-range proof removes a following negation guard after same-binding subtraction/division, zero multiplication, and division by a compile-time divisor whose successful quotient cannot be `i64::MIN`; `/ 1` deliberately retains the guard because it can still produce `i64::MIN`. Checked additive results now participate in that range proof as well: a successful addition with a positive compile-time operand, subtraction of a negative compile-time operand, or subtraction from a nonnegative compile-time left operand cannot produce `i64::MIN`, so a following unary negation lowers without a second guard. Opposite-signed forms that can still produce `i64::MIN` retain checked negation, and ordinary native plus dynamic UI lowering share the proof.
  Verified dynamic additive-inverse follow-up: direct same-binding operands now prove the outer checked operation redundant for `(x + y) - y`, `(y + x) - y`, `(x - y) + y`, `y + (x - y)`, and `x - (x - y)`. Lowering keeps the inner checked addition/subtraction authoritative, preserves the outer binding read and exact source evaluation structure, and emits the mathematically safe outer native operation without a second overflow helper in ordinary native and dynamic UI code. Unmatched dynamic operands retain the general checked path.
  Verified dynamic divide/multiply follow-up: `(value / divisor) * divisor` and `divisor * (value / divisor)` now remove the redundant outer multiplication overflow guard when the outer factor is the exact same direct binding as the checked inner divisor. The inner division remains authoritative for zero and `i64::MIN / -1`, effectful dividends still evaluate exactly once, unmatched factors retain `flux_mul_i64`, and ordinary native plus dynamic UI lowering share the proof.
  Verified dynamic multiply/divide follow-up: `(value * divisor) / divisor` and `(divisor * value) / divisor` now reuse the successful checked multiplication to prove the outer `i64::MIN / -1` overflow case impossible when the divisor is the exact same direct binding. Lowering retains only the required zero-divisor guard, preserves effectful multiplicands exactly once, keeps unmatched divisors on the full checked division path, and shares the proof across ordinary native and dynamic UI expressions.
  Verified arithmetic-identity equivalence follow-up: pure checked `i64` expression equivalence now normalizes the already-zero-cost identity wrappers `x + 0`, `0 + x`, `x - 0`, `x * 1`, `1 * x`, and `x / 1` before comparison/common-operand reuse. Reflexive comparisons and repeated checked arithmetic therefore evaluate one authoritative pure value instead of retaining redundant reads/comparisons merely because one spelling contains an identity wrapper; nested checked expressions and dynamic UI lowering share the proof, while calls/effectful expressions deliberately retain every source evaluation. Broader CFG/SSA common-subexpression proof coverage remains pending.
  Verified dynamic negated-additive inverse follow-up: direct binding negations now participate in proven-safe outer cancellation for `(value + offset) + -offset`, `-offset + (value + offset)`, `(value + -offset) + offset`, and the matching subtraction forms. The checked inner arithmetic and checked negation remain authoritative and source evaluations remain intact, while only the final overflow helper proven redundant is removed in ordinary native and dynamic UI lowering; unmatched bindings retain the general checked path.
  Verified partial constant additive-cancellation follow-up: a successful checked constant shift now carries enough one-dimensional range proof to eliminate a smaller opposite-direction outer shift, so forms such as `(value + 7) - 3`, `(value - 7) + 3`, and `3 + (value - 7)` keep the first overflow check but lower the second operation directly. The proof is magnitude-bounded, refuses unrepresentable signed shifts, preserves exact-once evaluation of effectful inner operands, and retains the general checked path for larger cancellation or same-direction shifts; ordinary native and dynamic UI lowering share the optimization.
  Verified multiplicative-result negation follow-up: after a checked multiplication by a compile-time factor that cannot produce `i64::MIN` (`0`, `-1`, or a factor whose absolute value is not a power of two), or after a checked direct same-binding square `value * value`, a following unary negation lowers directly without a second `i64::MIN` guard. A successful square is nonnegative, so it cannot equal `i64::MIN`; the multiplication overflow check remains authoritative. Factors such as `2`, `-2`, and other signed powers of two retain checked negation because a successful nonsquare product can still equal `i64::MIN`; ordinary native and dynamic UI lowering share the proof.
  Verified same-negated-binding follow-up: repeated direct checked negations now share the first proven result across `(-value) + (-value)`, subtraction, multiplication, and division. Addition and multiplication evaluate one checked negation into a compiler-owned expression-local value before applying the ordinary checked arithmetic helper to that value twice; subtraction returns zero after one guard, and division feeds the one checked result through the existing self-division zero guard. The `i64::MIN` negation trap plus downstream arithmetic/zero-divisor failures remain authoritative, duplicate negation work disappears, and ordinary native plus dynamic UI lowering share the proof.
  Verified checked-negated reflexive-comparison follow-up: `(-value)` compared reflexively against the same direct binding now evaluates one checked negation, preserves the `i64::MIN` overflow trap, and folds `==`/`<=`/`>=` to true plus `!=`/`<`/`>` to false without evaluating a redundant second negation. Ordinary native and dynamic UI lowering share the proof; broader CFG/SSA proof coverage remains pending.
  Verified pure checked-expression reflexive-comparison follow-up: structurally identical side-effect-free `i64` arithmetic trees built from direct bindings/constants plus checked negation/addition/subtraction/multiplication/division now evaluate only the left expression before folding `==`/`<=`/`>=` to true or `!=`/`<`/`>` to false. The first checked arithmetic evaluation remains authoritative for overflow/division failures, so duplicate guards disappear without suppressing the source-visible trap; nested expressions and dynamic UI lowering share the same proof. Calls and other effectful expressions deliberately remain outside this optimization; broader CFG/SSA common-subexpression proof coverage remains pending.
  Verified commutative checked-expression equivalence follow-up: side-effect-free checked `i64` addition and multiplication trees now recognize recursively commuted operands, so comparisons such as `(x + y) == (y + x)` and nested commuted `+`/`*` forms evaluate one authoritative checked tree before folding the proven reflexive result. Subtraction/division remain order-sensitive, calls and other effectful subtrees retain both source evaluations, dynamic UI lowering shares the proof, and native execution coverage pins both optimized results and preserved effects; broader CFG/SSA common-subexpression proof coverage remains pending.
  Verified equivalent checked-arithmetic operand reuse follow-up: arithmetic with two provably equivalent side-effect-free checked `i64` operands now evaluates one authoritative operand tree and reuses its value. Addition and multiplication retain the required outer overflow guard over the shared value, subtraction produces zero after the first checked evaluation, and division retains the self-division zero guard; recursively commuted `+`/`*` operand trees participate while calls/effectful trees remain unmerged. Ordinary native and dynamic UI lowering share the optimization; native execution coverage pins optimized results and observable effects while generated-C assertions pin the retained outer overflow/self-division guards. Broader CFG/SSA common-subexpression elimination remains pending.
  Verified checked-negation equivalence follow-up: equivalent checked negation spellings (`-value`, `0 - value`, `-1 * value`, and `value / -1`, including compile-time constant aliases) now share one authoritative checked operand evaluation when compared or reused by checked arithmetic. The surviving negation still traps on `i64::MIN`, outer arithmetic keeps any independently required overflow/zero-divisor guard, ordinary native and dynamic UI lowering share the proof, and effectful operands remain deliberately unmerged so source-visible evaluations are preserved. Broader CFG/SSA common-subexpression elimination remains pending.
  Verified constant-alias/range-proof follow-up: pure checked `i64` equivalence now compares compile-time constant values rather than requiring identical constant spellings, so equal aliases participate in commutative common-expression reuse. The same proof now establishes that equivalent checked subtraction, square, and self-division results cannot be `i64::MIN`, eliminating a following redundant negation guard while retaining the authoritative inner overflow/zero-divisor checks; ordinary native and dynamic UI lowering share the proof.
  Verified pure additive-inverse equivalence follow-up: `left + -right` and `-left + right` now collapse to zero when the non-negated operands are provably equivalent side-effect-free checked `i64` expressions, including identity wrappers, commuted pure arithmetic, and equivalent checked-negation spellings. Lowering evaluates only the authoritative checked-negation side, so `i64::MIN` still traps; calls/effectful expressions retain both source evaluations, and ordinary native plus dynamic UI lowering share the proof.
  - [x] Static list-cardinality proofs now propagate through compile-time slices (including clipped/negative/reverse bounds) and compiler-known `take` / `skip` views over known-length lists, so nested indexes erase their runtime bounds helper only when the resulting cardinality proves the index valid; runtime-dependent slice bounds/counts and statically out-of-range indexes retain checked lowering.
  - [x] Checked `i64` identity/strength-reduction arithmetic eliminates provably redundant runtime safety helpers in ordinary code and dynamic UI expressions: `x + 0`, `0 + x`, `x - 0`, `x * 1`, `1 * x`, and `x / 1` lower directly to the dynamic operand, while `0 - x`, `-1 * x`, `x * -1`, and `x / -1` lower through the single checked-negation guard instead of subtraction/multiplication/division helpers and therefore preserve the `i64::MIN` overflow trap. Unused checked helpers tree-shake while arithmetic that can still overflow or divide invalidly retains checked lowering; regression coverage pins the optimized forms and the retained `x + 1` overflow check.
  - [x] Checked `i64` zero-multiplication annihilators now erase the redundant multiplication overflow helper for `0 * x` and `x * 0` while preserving exact-once evaluation of the dynamic operand through the native expression, so calls and nested checked operations still execute/trap according to Flux semantics. Dead-store elimination reuses the proven trap-free `+ 0`, `- 0`, `* 0`, `* 1`, and `/ 1` cases when their dynamic operand is itself discardable, removing dead bindings instead of merely emitting their reduced expression; negating `0 - x`/`*-1`/`/-1` forms remain non-discardable because `i64::MIN` must still trap. Regression coverage pins the optimized forms, effect/trap boundaries, and retained inner checked arithmetic.
  - [x] Checked `i64` division by a compile-time non-zero divisor other than `-1` now lowers directly to native signed division because the compiler has proven both failure cases impossible: division by zero is excluded and `i64::MIN / -1` is handled by the existing checked-negation reduction. Compile-time constants and negative divisors participate in the proof, dynamic divisors retain `flux_div_i64`, and dead-store elimination may discard a proven-safe constant division only when its dynamic dividend is itself discardable.
  - [x] Same-binding checked subtraction now proves `x - x` is exactly zero without an overflow path for ordinary scalar locals/parameters and dynamic view-state expressions, lowers directly to `0` instead of `flux_sub_i64`, and is treated as discardable by dead-store elimination because reading the same scalar binding has no effects. Regression coverage pins native helper removal, UI-state lowering, and elimination of a semantically dead self-subtraction while unrelated checked subtraction remains guarded.
  - [x] Same-binding checked division now proves `x / x` cannot hit the signed `i64::MIN / -1` overflow case, so ordinary scalar locals/parameters and dynamic view-state expressions lower through a single zero-divisor guard that returns `1` for every non-zero value instead of the general two-condition division helper. The zero case still traps with the ordinary Flux invalid-division diagnostic, the specialized helper tree-shakes unless used, and regression coverage pins both native/UI lowering and retention of the zero safety check.
  - [x] Reflexive same-binding scalar comparisons now fold without runtime work: `x == x`, `x <= x`, and `x >= x` become `true`, while `x != x`, `x < x`, and `x > x` become `false` for source forms the type checker already accepts. This applies to ordinary functions and dynamic UI expressions, removes redundant primitive comparisons plus `strcmp`/error-equality calls for self-comparisons, and remains limited to direct same-binding reads so effectful expressions are never duplicated or discarded by the proof.
  - [x] Same-binding boolean short-circuit identities now erase redundant runtime work for direct local/view-state reads: `flag && flag` and `flag || flag` lower to one read of `flag`, preserving the same result and short-circuit semantics without emitting a second comparison operand. The proof is deliberately limited to direct same-binding reads so calls or other effectful expressions are never duplicated or discarded; regression coverage pins both ordinary native and dynamic UI lowering.
  - [x] Additive-inverse cancellation for direct `i64` bindings now removes the redundant checked-add helper from `x + -x` and `-x + x`, yielding zero after exactly one checked negation. The remaining negation guard deliberately preserves Flux's `i64::MIN` overflow trap, so the optimization removes only the safety work proven redundant rather than weakening checked arithmetic semantics; generated-C regression coverage pins both operand orders.
  - [x] Boolean identity/absorbing-constant lowering removes redundant short-circuit work for `value && true`, `value || false`, `true && value`, and `false || value`; `value && false` / `value || true` still evaluate the dynamic left operand exactly once before producing the proven result, while `false && value` / `true || value` erase the unreachable right operand entirely. Ordinary native and dynamic UI expressions share the same proof path, and regression coverage pins both eliminated work and preserved side effects.
  - [x] Complementary same-binding boolean proofs eliminate redundant short-circuit work for `flag && !flag`, `flag || !flag`, `!flag && flag`, and `!flag || flag`: lowering evaluates only the original left boolean read, erases the provably unnecessary right read/negation, and returns the proven constant result. The optimization is deliberately restricted to direct same-binding reads so effectful expressions are never duplicated or discarded; ordinary native and dynamic UI expressions share the same proof path and regression coverage.
  - [x] Negated same-binding boolean idempotence now removes duplicate work from `!flag && !flag` and `!flag || !flag`: direct binding reads are negated exactly once and reused as the result, while repeated calls or other effectful expressions remain untouched. Ordinary native and dynamic UI lowering share the same conservative proof and regression coverage.
- [x] LTO in native release builds through Clang `-flto`, covered by build-mode tests.
- [x] Continuous bootstrap performance harness with optimized overflow-safe Flux-vs-Rust/C compute, collection-pipeline, interface-dispatch, dynamic function-call/control-flow, and display-backed native-UI baselines, output-equivalence checks where applicable, median runtime/code-size reporting, configurable checked-Rust and native-UI release thresholds, C reference tracking, and a push/PR CI gate. Broader workloads can extend the same target-aware harness without reopening the current representative-performance contract.
- [x] Tree shaking/dead symbol elimination for all current bootstrap backend concepts: whole-program reachability removes unreachable functions, anonymous functions, value types/helpers, interface metadata/dispatch targets, enum/struct helpers, function-pointer typedefs, platform/runtime helpers, and dead view metadata while preserving public/dynamic roots; future backend resource kinds must join the same reachability graph when introduced.
  - [x] Bootstrap whole-program function reachability keeps `main`, public exports, interface implementation mappings, application/view callback references, and their transitive private callees while omitting provably unreachable private function prototypes/bodies and anonymous functions from generated C.
  - [x] Reachability now prunes unused private struct/enum definitions, individual unused enum-constructor and struct-update helpers, function-pointer typedefs, unused interface pack helpers and per-capability dynamic-dispatch/multi-return helpers, implementation mappings for interface capabilities that are never referenced, unreachable interface value-layout targets, metadata/callback/type roots from views the current native backend does not emit, background-process headers, and unused bootstrap runtime helper bodies while retaining public value types/contracts, every implementation needed by a genuinely dynamic capability, reachable function-local aggregate uses, recursive by-value dependencies, and all enum tags needed by reachable matching. Primitive `const` declarations are compile-time-only and therefore create no runtime storage/symbol roots.
  - [x] Closed-world dynamic interface target sets now come from typed IR interface-pack facts, so an implementation can be removed from dynamic dispatch even when its concrete type remains reachable for unrelated code; exported interface-valued entry points deliberately keep the target set open.
  - [x] Whole-program reachability consumes only CFG- and value-reachable typed IR: compile-time-dead statements and source-foldable unselected nested values no longer retain private callees, first-class named/anonymous functions, function-pointer typedefs, value types, interface packs, interface implementations, dispatch targets, enum-constructor helpers, or background-process runtime support merely because their checked AST/IR was constructed before reachability pruning.
  - [ ] Extend reachability pruning/specialization to future backend symbols/resources as those backend concepts are introduced.
- [ ] Remove bootstrap C as the semantic backend after parity is proven.

# Milestone 8 — Application model

- [x] `app` declaration / application entry model.
  - [x] Bootstrap `app ViewName` process entry selects one visible zero-parameter root view, is formatter/LSP-keyword aware, rejects competing `fn main`, and lowers through the current native Linux/Android application backends. Rich process arguments and multiple-window entry remain pending in their dedicated follow-up tracks rather than blocking the core application entry model.
- [x] Bootstrap application metadata: `app ViewName(id: ..., title: ..., width: ..., height: ..., resizable: ...)` accepts compile-time typed values/constants, validates positive dimensions and GLib-compatible reverse-DNS-style application IDs, formats canonically, and lowers identity/title/default size/resizability into the native Linux window. Icons/permissions remain in their dedicated later tracks rather than being hidden in this bootstrap surface.
- [x] Assets/resources: manifest-backed packages may declare a relative `[package].assets` directory, and portable `asset://path` image sources resolve from that package resource root. Linux development runs receive the source asset root directly while packaged Linux layouts copy assets beside the executable; Android APK/AAB builds embed the same tree as native package assets and load it through `AssetManager`. Asset directories are constrained to the package root and symlink traversal is rejected during packaging, without exposing resource-manager objects to Flux source.
- [x] Environment/configuration: portable application build-time configuration uses package-scoped primitive `[constants]` from `flux.toml` through explicit `package.name` references that fold away before native lowering and may feed application metadata/view properties; desktop/server runtime deployment configuration uses typed `process.hasEnv` / `process.env` host-environment access, while Android intentionally keeps configuration in package/application metadata and typed platform capabilities rather than inventing Unix-style environment semantics.
- [x] Platform permissions for the current native application targets: Android packages accept validated declarative `[android].permissions`, generate deterministic manifest entries, expose typed `android.allowed` / `android.ask` runtime capabilities, and infer compiler-owned vibration/notification permissions only when reachable code needs them; Linux has no equivalent application-manifest permission surface to configure. Permission JNI/runtime support tree-shakes when unused and remains hidden behind Flux APIs rather than application bridge code.
- [ ] Lifecycle events as functions/capabilities, not class callbacks.
  - [x] Bootstrap `app` metadata accepts named typed `onStart`, `onResume`, `onPause`, `onStop`, and `onExit` `fn() -> void` callbacks. Linux maps start/stop/exit to native application activation/shutdown and resume/pause to native window active-state changes; Android maps the same callbacks directly onto `ANativeActivityCallbacks`, with no application/controller object exposed to Flux source.
  - [x] Windows configuration notifications now dispatch the typed `onConfigurationChanged: fn() -> void` callback directly from native `WM_SETTINGCHANGE` / `WM_DISPLAYCHANGE` messages and refresh the existing controls in place; generated Win32 lowering is regression-tested. Portable saved-state/configuration parity and richer lifecycle context remain tracked below.
  - [x] Verified saved-state bound enforcement: Linux save callbacks use the shared bounded C-string scan and Android save callbacks scan only through the documented 16 MiB limit before copying, so native lifecycle serialization cannot perform an unbounded `strlen` read; generated-code regression coverage pins both paths.
  - [ ] Complete suspension/restoration and lifecycle context beyond the current Android process-recreation/configuration hooks: Android exposes typed `onConfigurationChanged` / `onLowMemory` callbacks plus compiler-owned `onSaveState: fn() -> str` / `onRestoreState: fn(str) -> void` process-recreation state, while Linux now honors `onConfigurationChanged` after updating compiler-owned window environment bindings and continues to reject the Android-only `onLowMemory` callback. Linux and Windows persist bounded saved state across launches, and the Android native activity bridge now applies the same 16 MiB saved-state bound on both serialization and restoration. Portable saved-state/configuration parity, explicit suspension semantics, multiple-window lifecycle, and capability-rich lifecycle context remain pending.
- [x] Background/foreground transitions for current application targets: typed `onResume` / `onPause` lifecycle callbacks map to GTK window active-state changes on Linux and native Activity resume/pause callbacks on Android, preserving the function/capability model without controller objects.
- [ ] Multiple windows where the target supports them.
- [x] Platform capability discovery for the current native application targets: target-specific capabilities remain ordinary typed functions rather than runtime platform objects, and unsupported target reachability still fails at code generation while unreachable platform-only code tree-shakes safely.
  - [x] Android exposes `android.sdk() -> i64` through the NDK plus `android.feature(feature: str) -> bool` through compiler-owned direct `PackageManager.hasSystemFeature` JNI. Ordinary Flux control flow can therefore gate behavior by API level or any stable Android system-feature identifier without reflection, a plugin registry, or user-authored bridge code; both helpers tree-shake when unreachable and participate in LSP completion/signature help.

# Milestone 9 — UI language and flat layout

The UI model must avoid Flutter-style deeply nested widget construction and object hierarchies. Sequencing note: the compiler-backed surface syntax is being prototyped early, before rendering backends, because the flat layout grammar is a core language constraint and should not be retrofitted after a widget-tree API takes shape.

- [x] `view` declarations/functions for the flat declarative UI model.
  - [x] Bootstrap top-level `view Name { ... }` declarations with visibility, source spans, formatter support, project merging, and semantic symbols.
  - [x] Parameterized `view Name(data: Type, optional: Type = default) { ... }` declarations: parameters are statically typed, defaults use the ordinary constant-default rules, view bodies can reference parameters, and parameter symbols retain editor spans/types.
  - [x] Additional view-producing function composition is intentionally omitted from the current language: parameterized views already compose as typed flat elements, while arbitrary view-returning functions would add builder-style construction without a demonstrated capability gap. Reopen only if a concrete use case cannot be expressed through typed data/view composition.
- [x] Views compose through typed data rather than subclassing: a declared Flux view can be used as another view's flat sibling element type, arguments are named properties checked against the target view's parameters/defaults, module visibility is enforced, and recursive composition cycles are rejected.
- [x] Flat element declarations.
  - [x] Bootstrap sibling element syntax (`Type name at row,column`) with unique local names and optional property expressions; nested child elements are syntactically rejected.
- [x] Grid-first layout.
  - [x] Bootstrap grid grammar with explicit columns/rows, logical fixed units, `fr` tracks, `auto`, gap, explicit padding, explicit row/column placement, and row/column spans lowers to the current native Linux/Android layouts. Common minimum sizing, margins/alignment, stable placement checks, and responsive property expressions are implemented; responsive track definitions, max/intrinsic sizing, and optional named areas remain explicit follow-up work.
  - [ ] Statically type/check element properties against built-in or interface-defined element contracts instead of treating property expressions as untyped view metadata.
    - [x] Bootstrap built-in element contracts validate known property names and property expression types, including typed function callbacks, without introducing widget classes; semantic symbols expose the property type for editor tooling.
    - [x] Declared Flux views act as custom element contracts derived directly from their typed parameters/defaults; custom-view property types flow into semantic/editor symbols.
