- [x] Design tokens/variables: compiler-owned semantic color/typography plus spacing/radius/elevation/motion tokens provide the polished shared defaults, while ordinary typed top-level `const` values are the zero-cost user-defined design-variable mechanism for reusable dimensions and color strings. Native lowering folds both mechanisms to constants, so Flux does not need a second theme-variable object/runtime or dedicated token declaration language.
- [x] System theme integration for the current native backends: Linux `theme: "system"` preserves the GTK/system preference, while Android resolves the current `Configuration.UI_MODE_NIGHT_*` mode before Activity creation and reapplies it on `uiMode` configuration changes; explicit `light`/`dark` select the matching native `DeviceDefault` theme without bundling a framework theme runtime.
- [x] Device/window/orientation queries for the current native application backends; richer monitor/work-area/device-class queries remain follow-up extensions rather than blocking the core environment surface.
  - [x] Bootstrap root-view environment exposes read-only typed `windowWidth: i64`, `windowHeight: i64`, `windowIsLandscape: bool`, `windowIsPortrait: bool`, and `displayScale: i64` bindings directly inside ordinary view property expressions. Linux seeds them from the application/default window geometry and native widget scale, watches `GtkWindow` default-width/default-height plus widget scale-factor changes, and reuses the existing fine-grained refresh path on ordinary resizes without exposing a window object to Flux source. Android converts `DisplayMetrics` pixels through the exact floating-point density before exposing logical width/height, keeps the integer `displayScale` compatibility binding separate, and uses the exact density for native dp-style layout/styling so non-integer-density devices do not inflate spacing, radii, fixed tracks, or translations. Exact maximized/fullscreen content geometry, monitor/work-area/device-class queries, and richer device-class semantics remain pending.
- [x] Adaptive components where platform differences are valuable for the current native application backends: Flux keeps one semantic source contract while the compiler chooses native controls/emphasis appropriate to Linux and Android. Semantic `Toggle` now keeps the native labeled `GtkCheckButton` on Linux but lowers to `android.widget.Switch` on Android with theme-aware thumb/track colors through the shared `CompoundButton` callback/accessibility/refresh contract; future adaptive substitutions should be added only when a concrete platform capability or UX difference warrants them.
  - [x] Bootstrap responsive property adaptation can use the read-only window/orientation/scale environment in ordinary conditional expressions, so native element text/visibility/enabled/accessibility and other already state-aware properties can react to window changes without a controller or widget rebuild. Responsive grid-track definitions, component substitution, and compile-time-only style properties remain pending.
  - [x] Bootstrap `Button.primary: bool` maps an explicit compile-time primary action to native platform emphasis without exposing platform widget APIs in Flux source: Linux uses GTK's `suggested-action` style class, while Android resolves the active native theme accent, applies it through the platform button tint surface, derives contrasting label text, keeps an explicit lower-emphasis disabled color state, and preserves ordinary Android pressed/focus/accessibility behavior.
- [x] Animation timelines through compiler-owned `frame.timeline(durationMs, callback)`: a non-negative `i64` duration drives an exact `fn(i64) -> void` callback with normalized `0..1000` progress on native frames, including a final `1000` callback. Linux lowers directly to the GTK frame clock and Android to `View.postOnAnimation` through compiler-generated JNI/Activity glue; invalid constant durations/callback shapes fail statically, dynamic negative durations fail safely, LSP completion/signature help expose the API, and unused timeline support tree-shakes completely without a controller/animation-object runtime.
- [x] Easing/springs.
  - [x] Bootstrap property transitions accept compile-time `transitionEasing` values `linear`, `ease`, `easeIn`, `easeOut`, `easeInOut`, `spring`, `springGentle`, and `springSnappy`. Ordinary easing maps to native GTK timing functions; the spring roles map to compiler-owned overshooting cubic-bezier curves with no animation object/runtime allocation. Static checking rejects unsupported/non-constant easing names, LSP value completion exposes the canonical roles, and the existing reduced-motion override remains authoritative.
- [x] Property transitions.
  - [x] Every built-in element accepts non-negative compile-time `transitionMs` plus optional `transitionDelayMs` / `transitionEasing`. Linux lowers these to standard GTK CSS transitions on `all` animatable properties; this already animates state-driven transform changes without an animation object model. Reduced-motion policy and selective transition-property syntax remain separate follow-up work.
- [x] Layout transitions for state-driven visibility reflow: common compile-time `layoutTransitionMs` opts flat native elements into allocation/placement animation without exposing an animation/controller object. Linux lowers opted-in elements through compiler-owned `GtkRevealer` allocation transitions, Android installs the platform `LayoutTransition` on the root grid, one deterministic duration is required per view for portable behavior, and views that omit the property generate no layout-transition machinery.
- [x] Gesture-driven animation for direct native manipulation: common compile-time `dragTranslate: true` and `pinchScale: true` properties bind the existing portable drag/pinch recognizers directly into each element's native translate/scale transform state on Linux and Android, compose with explicit base transforms, refresh the existing native control in place, and require no gesture/controller object or application-authored bridge. Named `onDrag` / `onScale` callbacks remain independently composable with the same gesture so application logic can observe direct manipulation without owning the rendering plumbing.
- [x] Frame synchronization through compiler-owned `frame.next(callback: fn() -> void)`: the callback is strictly typed, one-shot, application-only, and tree-shaken when unused; Linux schedules it on the active GTK widget frame clock with `gtk_widget_add_tick_callback`, while Android posts it for the next UI frame through `View.postOnAnimation` and compiler-generated JNI/Activity glue. No frame/controller object or application-authored platform bridge is exposed, and LSP completion/signature help surface the portable capability.
- [x] Reduced-motion accessibility handling.
  - [x] Native property-transition styles include a `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce` override that removes transition duration/delay, and each transition CSS provider live-binds GTK's system `gtk-interface-reduced-motion` setting into its `prefers-reduced-motion` property. The application therefore follows the platform accessibility preference without a Flux-specific toggle.

# Milestone 15 — Android target

Android is the first application-platform target. Android integration must feel like programming Android in Flux, not embedding a second Kotlin/Java application beside it: Flux code can directly use Android-specific modules for capabilities such as notifications, WorkManager/background work, vibration, intents, services, media, and sensors. The backend should prefer direct NDK/native APIs when Android provides them; for JVM/AndroidX-only framework APIs it generates the necessary Kotlin/Java bytecode/classes and JNI/native entry thunks automatically. No method-channel equivalent, hand-written JNI shim, serialized plugin call protocol, or user-maintained bridge package is required in ordinary Flux application code.

- [x] Android target triple/backend.
  - [x] Bootstrap target-aware codegen lowers a Flux `app` to an NDK `NativeActivity` shared library for `arm64-v8a`, `x86_64`, or `armeabi-v7a`; ABI + minSdk select the matching NDK Clang target and Android builds do not pull in the Linux/GTK backend. Application source stays Java/Kotlin/JNI-free; when Android framework APIs require JVM-side types, Flux may generate compiler-owned runtime classes and direct JNI thunks. Real arm64 debug APK, x86_64 release APK, and armeabi-v7a profile APK artifacts have been produced on Nox, and the native library exports `ANativeActivity_onCreate` as the framework lifecycle entry point.
- [x] `flux build android`.
  - [x] Manifest-backed packages build directly through the installed SDK/NDK with `--abi`, `--mode`, and optional `-o`; no Gradle project, Kotlin application, method channel, or user-authored JNI shim is generated.
  - [x] `flux run android`.
  - [x] The command builds the selected ABI, installs the resulting APK, and launches its concrete exported activity. Runtime selection is ABI-aware: matching ADB devices are preferred, but a running matching Waydroid container is selected instead of attempting an incompatible ADB install; known mismatches fail before installation. `--device <adb-serial>|waydroid` explicitly disambiguates hosts with multiple runtimes, and omitting `--abi` probes that selected runtime before compilation. Device discovery/ABI probes are time-bounded, explicit-device runs skip unrelated transports, and install/launch operations now fail with actionable timeouts instead of hanging indefinitely on a stale ADB/Waydroid connection. ADB launches use the exact compiler-selected `FluxActivity` or framework `NativeActivity` component with `am start -W` instead of a package-only implicit intent, so launcher resolution failures cannot be reported as successful runs. The command has been exercised end to end on Nox through build → install → explicit launch → live/resumed-process verification on x86_64 Waydroid while arm64 ADB devices were also connected.
- [x] APK output.
  - [x] Bootstrap packaging generates the binary manifest, bundles `lib/<abi>/libflux.so`, zip-aligns the artifact, signs it, and verifies the final APK. Nox validation confirmed v1/v2/v3 signatures, minSdk/targetSdk metadata, the NativeActivity launcher, native ABI declaration, and the packaged Flux shared library.
- [x] AAB output.
  - [x] `flux build android ... --format aab` compiles all supported ABIs, converts the generated manifest/resource table to protobuf form with `aapt2`, assembles a valid base module with bundletool, signs the resulting App Bundle with the compiler-managed development key, and verifies the bundle. Nox validation confirmed bundletool accepts the release AAB and sees arm64-v8a, x86_64, and armeabi-v7a native libraries.
- [x] Debug/profile/release variants.
  - [x] Android uses the same explicit optimization contracts as native builds: debug (`-O0`, full debug), profile (`-O2`, debug/frame pointers), and release (`-O3`, LTO). All three modes have produced Android artifacts on Nox.
- [x] Android manifest generation.
  - [x] Flux generates package/version/SDK/application/launcher metadata itself rather than requiring an application-authored Android manifest; packages with native View rendering point at the compiler-generated `FluxActivity` subclass while headless/native-only bootstrap shapes can retain the framework `NativeActivity` directly. The generated Java-to-DEX step includes every compiler-emitted class (including nested/helper classes) rather than assuming the activity is a single `.class`, preventing runtime class omissions as compiler-owned Android glue grows.
- [x] Package/application ID configuration.
  - [x] Optional `[android].application_id` accepts a validated lowercase reverse-DNS identifier; otherwise Flux derives a deterministic `app.flux.<package>` ID from the package name.
- [x] minSdk/targetSdk configuration.
  - [x] Optional integer `[android].min_sdk` / `target_sdk` fields are validated (`minSdk >= 21`, target >= min): minSdk drives the NDK ABI compiler floor, targetSdk remains the generated manifest/application compatibility target, and compiler-generated JVM glue compiles against the newest installed Android SDK platform that is at least as new as targetSdk. This keeps runtime-gated newer framework calls buildable without silently raising the application's targetSdk; bootstrap defaults remain minSdk 23 / targetSdk 36, matching the Google Play phone/tablet target-API requirement effective August 31, 2026 while older targets remain explicitly selectable where needed.
- [x] Signing configuration.
  - [x] Development signing is compiler-owned: Flux creates/reuses its own debug keystore, signs aligned APKs with `apksigner`, signs AABs with `jarsigner`, and verifies the results.
  - [x] Release builds may configure `[android].keystore` plus `[android].key_alias`; relative keystore paths resolve from the package root, while passwords stay out of source and are supplied through `FLUX_ANDROID_KEYSTORE_PASSWORD` with optional `FLUX_ANDROID_KEY_PASSWORD` override. APK signing uses apksigner environment-password sources and AAB signing uses jarsigner environment-password sources so credentials do not appear in command arguments. Fresh-key Nox E2E builds verified both release formats against the configured signing certificate; unsigned/missing-key and missing-credential paths fail explicitly. Play App Signing can consume the resulting signed upload AAB without requiring a Flux-specific credential store.
- [x] Android lifecycle integration.
  - [x] Headless/native-only bootstrap apps wire typed Flux `app` `onStart`, `onResume`, `onPause`, `onStop`, `onExit`, `onConfigurationChanged`, and `onLowMemory` callbacks directly through `ANativeActivityCallbacks`; View-backed apps route the same lifecycle through the compiler-generated `FluxActivity` and direct JNI thunks. Both paths clear compiler-owned activity handles on destruction.
  - [x] Process-recreation state is compiler-owned and typed on both Android entry paths: `onSaveState: fn() -> str` returns an app-defined payload and `onRestoreState: fn(str) -> void` receives it after recreation. NativeActivity uses its saved-state buffer directly; generated View-backed activities persist the same payload through `Bundle` while application code contains no JNI/JVM serialization shim.
- [x] Activity/window integration hidden behind Flux application APIs.
  - [x] Flux source continues to declare only `app ViewName(...)`, with no Activity subclass or JNI bootstrap in application code. The compiler uses framework `NativeActivity` for native-only shapes and generates the minimal ordinary `FluxActivity`/Dex/JNI boundary when Android Views are required, including configuration-change handling and native lifecycle routing.
- [x] Compiler-owned Android interop lowering: current Android platform calls use direct NDK/C ABI lowering where available (`android.sdk()`), framework-only APIs share one compiler-emitted JNI attach/release boundary, generated `FluxActivity`/DEX native declarations route required JVM-side integration without application bridge code, and framework callbacks (including lifecycle/UI/deep-link callbacks) return directly into compiled Flux functions. Broader binding/API coverage remains tracked by the platform-module/FFI items below rather than reopening the interop mechanism.
  - [x] Framework-bound intrinsics share one compiler-emitted JNI attachment boundary: `android.vibrate(durationMs)` calls the vibrator service, `android.open(url)` constructs/launches an Android `ACTION_VIEW` intent, `android.share(text)` constructs a native `ACTION_SEND` chooser, `android.clipboard(text)` writes native plain-text clipboard data through `ClipboardManager`/`ClipData`, `android.showKeyboard()` / `android.hideKeyboard()` control the focused native view through `InputMethodManager`, generic runtime permissions call `checkSelfPermission`/`requestPermissions`, and notification APIs directly use `NotificationManager`, `NotificationChannel`, `Notification.Builder`, runtime permission methods, and `PendingIntent`, including compiler-owned UTF-8→Java string conversion. No application JNI/Kotlin/Java bridge source or serialized method-channel protocol is generated. Non-Android native builds reject reachable `android.*` calls during code generation while unreachable platform-only functions can be tree-shaken safely.
- [x] First-class target-specific `android.*` Flux modules generated/maintained from stable platform bindings where practical, with compile-time API-level/target checks and no reflection-based runtime dispatch requirement. Android packaging separates the configured targetSdk from the compile-platform API used for compiler-generated JVM glue, automatically choosing the newest installed SDK platform at least as new as targetSdk so runtime-gated newer framework references do not fail in javac merely because the application intentionally targets an older API. One centralized binding table now drives `android.*` static argument/return checking, LSP completion/signature help, reachable-call minSdk validation, native runtime-symbol selection, optional focus-wrap lowering, and generated call return shapes. Target-specific NDK/JNI implementations remain direct and tree-shakeable without a reflection dispatcher or user-authored bridge.
  - [x] Bootstrap `android` platform namespace participates in ordinary static checking plus LSP qualified completion/signature help; current members include `android.sdk()`, vibration, URL/share intents, native clipboard writes, keyboard show/hide control, programmatic focus next/prior/first/last/blur, focused-text selection/caret query/update, generic permission query/request, notification-channel creation, notification-permission query/request, plain/action notifications, notification cancellation, keep-awake window control, microphone recording, secure storage, and explicit activity finishing. `sdk` lowers directly through the NDK rather than JNI, giving target-specific code a typed runtime API-level gate. Broader generated bindings, compile-time availability metadata, and nested platform namespaces remain pending.
  - [x] `android.awake(enabled)` is a typed, tree-shakeable window capability with no user bridge: real `NativeActivity` entry paths lower to `ANativeActivity_setWindowFlags`/`AWINDOW_FLAG_KEEP_SCREEN_ON`, while compiler-generated View-backed activities use the direct framework `Window.setFlags` boundary because Android's NDK helper requires the framework-owned native activity backing object. Completion/signature help expose the API and unreachable calls emit neither path.
  - [x] `android.finish()` explicitly finishes the current Android activity without a user bridge: real `NativeActivity` entry paths lower directly to `ANativeActivity_finish`, while compiler-generated View-backed activities call framework `Activity.finish()` through the shared compiler-owned JNI boundary. Static checking, completion/signature help, and reachability-driven tree shaking cover the capability so unused builds emit neither implementation.
- [x] Native rendering/input backend.
  - [x] Bootstrap flat-grid Android rendering/input slice: the compiler emits a native `GridLayout` with native `TextView`/`Button` children, row/column spans, compile-time text, and direct named `Button.onPress` callback dispatch into compiled Flux functions. Packaging automatically generates/compiles a tiny `FluxActivity`/Dex implementing the Android listener interface, with no application-authored Java/Kotlin/JNI, Gradle project, method channel, serialization bus, or plugin registry. Nox validation produced a signed x86_64 APK containing `classes.dex` + `libflux.so`, installed/launched it in running Waydroid, and produced a signed all-ABI AAB containing `base/dex/classes.dex` plus arm64-v8a/x86_64/armeabi-v7a native libraries.
  - [x] Extend the Android renderer to functional view-state transitions/refresh, dynamic properties, remaining built-in controls, richer grid sizing/gaps, styling, keyboard/focus, and production input semantics.
    - [x] Android views now own `bool`/`i64` state plus `i64`/`bool`/`str` derived values; ordinary state-transition events recompute derived values and refresh existing native controls by compiler-owned stable element ID instead of rebuilding the hierarchy, and `text`, `visible`, and `enabled` expressions can read state, derived values, and live window/display environment bindings. Configuration changes refresh display metrics and may rebuild the view when layout/native configuration must be recreated.
    - [x] Native `TextInput`, `Toggle`, and `Radio` controls lower to `EditText`, `CheckBox`, and `RadioButton`; callbacks return directly into compiled Flux, typed text-change/IME-submit handlers receive `str`, `TextInput.multiline` enables native multiline editing, and compile-time `submitOnEnter` explicitly chooses whether Enter/IME Done submits (defaulting off for multiline and on for single-line). Toggle/radio state transitions refresh existing controls in place, and compiler-managed text/selection/focus caching preserves user input, caret/selection/composition, and focused element identity across the remaining full rebuild paths.
    - [x] Android grid lowering now honors fractional track weights, fixed logical-unit tracks, row/column spans, scaled grid padding, inter-cell gaps, per-element margins/minimum sizes, and start/center/end/fill alignment. Logical dimensions use Android's exact floating-point display density rather than a rounded scale factor, preventing systematic over/undersizing on common non-integer-density devices.
    - [x] First Android style slice: native controls support logical-unit padding, background color, solid border color/width, shared corner radius, and `Text` color/size/bold/italic/underline/strikethrough through compiler-generated framework code. `#RRGGBBAA` colors are translated correctly to Android ARGB at the framework boundary.
    - [x] Android common border parity now includes independent top/end/bottom/start widths and colors, common-color/width fallback, per-corner radii, and `none`/`solid`/`dashed`/`dotted`/`double` styles through one compiler-owned native drawable. The same per-edge color properties also lower to native GTK CSS on Linux.
    - [x] Android native shadows now honor compile-time `shadowColor`, non-negative `shadowBlur`, and signed X/Y offsets in the compiler-owned native drawable without changing Flux layout sizing; root native grids disable child/padding clipping so visual effects are not intentionally cropped at the container boundary.
    - [x] Native `Image` lowers to `ImageView` with typed `source`, `fit`, `alt`, and `canShrink` behavior; state/derived image sources refresh through the compiler-owned in-place refresh path. Nox E2E validation built and launched the resulting x86_64 APK in Waydroid.
    - [x] Android parity now covers native translate/rotate/scale transforms with configurable transform origin, per-corner radii, richer Text layout (`fontFamily`, letter spacing, line-height percentage, alignment, wrapping strategy, ellipsize, max lines), and direct tap/focus/blur/hover/leave callbacks through generated listener glue.
    - [x] Ordinary Android functional transitions use generated in-place native refresh rather than full `setContentView` reconstruction: every flat element receives a stable compiler-owned ID, refresh looks up the existing native control and reapplies refreshable dynamic properties, and programmatic Toggle/Radio selection is listener-suppressed so refresh cannot recursively dispatch Flux events.
    - [x] Finish higher-level composition controls, dynamic styling, and remaining production-grade focus/selection/text-composition/input edge cases. Portable `onTap` now uses Android's native click-listener path instead of raw touch-up dispatch, so pointer, keyboard, and accessibility `performClick` activation converge on one platform action; tappable views are explicitly focusable and native long-click consumption prevents a completed long press from also firing tap. State/derived-driven `Text.color` now joins the existing dynamic typography refresh path: validated hexadecimal or semantic colors update the existing native `TextView` in place, runtime values are checked before crossing the framework color parser, and the validator tree-shakes completely from static-color builds. State/derived-driven `Text.maxWidthChars` also refreshes the existing `TextView` in place, validates the native integer range, preserves the semantic typography default at construction, and treats runtime `0` as removing the previous character-width cap rather than forcing a hierarchy rebuild. State/derived-driven common `minWidth` and `minHeight` now likewise apply at initial Android construction and refresh the existing native View in place after state transitions, with runtime native-range checks and no hierarchy rebuild or application-authored bridge. State/derived-driven side-specific `borderTopWidth`, `borderEndWidth`, `borderBottomWidth`, and `borderStartWidth` now lower and refresh independently on the existing compiler-owned drawable, inherit dynamic common `borderWidth` only when the side is unspecified, retain native-range validation, and require no hierarchy rebuild or user-written bridge. State/derived-driven `borderTopColor`, `borderEndColor`, `borderBottomColor`, and `borderStartColor` now follow the same in-place path: each side evaluates and validates independently, explicit side colors override the common border color, unspecified sides retain common/default fallback, and state-only side-color transitions participate in refresh dependency tracking without rebuilding the native hierarchy or adding a platform bridge. Portable state/derived-driven `Image.fit` now refreshes the existing native image control in place on Linux and Android: runtime values are validated against `fill`/`contain`/`cover`/`scaleDown`, GTK updates `GtkPicture` content fit directly, and Android updates native `ImageView.ScaleType` through compiler-owned direct framework glue without reloading a static image source or adding application-authored bridge code. The compiler-declared semantic `Nav`, `Chart`, `Card`, `Header`, and `Content` built-ins now also lower to native GTK `GtkLabel`/Android `TextView` surfaces instead of being accepted by type checking but rejected by application codegen; their `label`/`title`/`text` values may be state/derived-driven and refresh the existing native control in place on both current backends. Controlled Android `TextInput.text` refresh now completes the current production-input/composition slice: external functional state changes update the existing `EditText` value in place, suppress feedback through the native text watcher, preserve selection when possible, and leave active IME composition untouched when the controlled value already matches the user's composing text.
      Portable state/derived-driven `alignX` / `alignY` now update native cell alignment in place on Linux and Android: runtime values are validated against `start`/`center`/`end`/`fill`, GTK mutates the existing widget alignment, Android updates the existing `GridLayout.LayoutParams` gravity and reapplies it, and static-only alignment retains direct constant lowering with no hierarchy rebuild or application-authored bridge. Portable `clip: bool` now has Android parity for the existing flat native elements: initial values call native `View.setClipToOutline` directly and state/derived changes refresh that same View in place, with no hierarchy rebuild, method channel, plugin registry, or application-authored bridge. State/derived-driven `Button.primary` now refreshes native emphasis in place on both current application backends: GTK toggles the native `suggested-action` class, while Android refreshes native button tint/text emphasis and updates compiler-owned shape drawables between semantic accent/raised fills without overriding an explicit `backgroundColor`; the hierarchy stays intact and no application-authored platform bridge is introduced.
      - [x] Android state/derived-driven `Text` emphasis styling refreshes `bold`, `italic`, `underline`, and `strikethrough` in place through the existing native `TextView` helper; refresh can clear underline/strike flags when values turn false, preserves semantic default bold when unspecified, and adds no application-authored platform bridge or hierarchy rebuild.
      - [x] Android state/derived-driven `Text.size` lowers through the existing native `TextView` refresh path without rebuilding the hierarchy; compile-time sizes retain direct constant lowering, while runtime values are checked for the positive Android text-size range before calling `setTextSize`, preserving native scalable-pixel semantics with no application bridge. State/derived-driven `Button.size` now follows the same in-place native sizing model on Android and Linux, with positive 32-bit runtime bounds, direct constant lowering for static sizes, and no hierarchy rebuild or application-authored bridge.
      - [x] Android state/derived-driven `Text.fontFamily` refreshes the existing native `TextView` typeface in place through compiler-owned direct JNI/framework calls; compile-time families keep their static validation, runtime values reject empty strings before crossing JNI, and no application-authored Kotlin/Java/JNI bridge or hierarchy rebuild is introduced.
      - [x] Android state/derived-driven `Text.letterSpacing` and `Text.lineHeightPercent` refresh the existing native `TextView` layout in place through the same compiler-owned `styleTextLayout` boundary; runtime spacing preserves the native-range guard, runtime line height remains positive and 32-bit bounded, multiple dynamic layout metrics coalesce into one refresh call, and no application-authored bridge or hierarchy rebuild is introduced.
      - [x] Android state/derived-driven `Text.textAlign`, `Text.wrapMode`, `Text.ellipsize`, and `Text.maxLines` now refresh the existing native `TextView` in place through that same compiler-owned `styleTextLayout` boundary, with runtime enum/range validation before JNI. Android refresh dependency tracking now also includes every already-supported dynamic Text color/size/emphasis/layout property, so a state-only style change cannot emit refresh code yet be skipped by the changed-state filter; no hierarchy rebuild, method channel, plugin registry, or application-authored platform bridge is introduced.
      - [x] Android state/derived-driven common `backgroundColor` refreshes the existing native `View` in place through compiler-owned direct framework glue. - [x] Accessibility/semantics bridge for the current Android/native-control surface: Flux accessibility labels/descriptions, hidden state, semantic roles, and explicit assistive traversal order lower onto the real platform controls through compiler-owned glue, with integrated Linux/Android regression coverage proving the native bridge rather than a parallel semantics tree. Richer named/custom accessibility actions remain tracked separately in Milestone 20.
  - [x] Bootstrap semantic text lands on native Android Views: canonical `accessibilityLabel` and `accessibilityDescription` lower to compiler-owned `contentDescription` generation, while `tooltip` uses the platform tooltip API when available.   - [x] Common typed `accessibilityHidden: bool` explicitly removes native elements and descendants from the accessibility tree without changing visual visibility: Linux lowers to `GTK_ACCESSIBLE_STATE_HIDDEN`, Android to `IMPORTANT_FOR_ACCESSIBILITY_NO_HIDE_DESCENDANTS`, and state-driven Android values refresh in place. Roles/actions/richer state semantics remain pending.
- [x] Notifications callable directly from Flux, including channels/actions where supported, with compiler-generated framework interop where required.
  - [x] `android.channel`, `android.noticeAllowed`, `android.askNotice`, `android.notify`, `android.notifyUrl`, and `android.cancelNotice` lower directly through compiler-owned framework JNI. API 33+ `POST_NOTIFICATIONS` is inferred into the generated manifest only when reachable code requires it; URL actions use an immutable/updateable Android `PendingIntent` without application-authored Java/Kotlin/JNI. Nox validation builds the action-enabled example as a signed x86_64 release APK, installs/launches it in Waydroid, and builds the all-ABI release AAB.
  Android `TextInput.keyboardType`, `password`, and `maxLength` may now be state/derived-driven: refresh recomputes and validates native input type and length filters on the existing `EditText`, preserves selection across input-type changes, and adds no hierarchy rebuild or application-authored bridge.
- [x] WorkManager/background-job integration callable directly from Flux; generate required `Worker`/`ListenableWorker` JVM entry classes and route execution into compiled Flux without user-authored Kotlin/Java glue. Typed `android.work(jobId, delayMs)` / `android.cancelWork(jobId)` lower directly through a generated `FluxWorkManagerWorker extends Worker` and the shared `FluxBackgroundRunner` JNI callback used by the existing `JobService`; generated enqueue/cancel use unique WorkManager work names. The compiler now resolves WorkManager 2.11.2 plus its transitive Maven runtime, downloads/caches required JAR/AAR artifacts under the Flux cache, extracts AAR bytecode, and feeds that deterministic classpath into both `javac` and D8 without generating a Gradle project; `FLUX_ANDROID_WORKMANAGER_CLASSPATH` remains an explicit override. Nox validation built a fresh x86_64 APK without the override and installed/launched it successfully in Waydroid, exercising WorkManager initialization from ordinary Flux source with no user-authored Kotlin/Java/JNI bridge.
- [x] Device vibration/haptics callable directly from Flux through the appropriate Android native/framework API for the configured API level.
  - [x] `android.vibrate(durationMs)` works from ordinary Flux code, tree-shakes its JNI helper when unused, automatically adds `android.permission.VIBRATE` only when reachable, and has been compiled into signed APK/AAB artifacts on Nox; the APK also installs/launches in Waydroid without application-authored bridge code.
- [x] Intents/deep links for the current Android application surface.
  - [x] Outbound URL/settings intents use compiler-owned direct framework interop: `android.open(url)` creates an `ACTION_VIEW` intent, `android.settings()` opens application details, and `android.noticeSettings()` opens app-specific notification settings on API 26+ with an older-device fallback. Inbound routing is likewise compiler-owned: `[android].deep_links` declares validated URI prefixes that become native `VIEW`/`DEFAULT`/`BROWSABLE` manifest filters, while typed application `onOpenUrl: fn(str) -> void` receives both the launch URL and subsequent `singleTop` intents through the generated Activity/JNI boundary. Application source needs no Activity subclass, Kotlin/Java/JNI bridge, method channel, serialization bus, or plugin registry; broader third-party/native intent interoperability remains under the platform-binding/FFI track.
- [x] Share sheet.
  - [x] `android.share(text)` opens the native Android chooser with an `ACTION_SEND` `text/plain` intent and compiler-owned UTF-8/JNI glue; Flux application code contains no Kotlin/Java/JNI bridge code.
- [x] Permissions.
  - [x] Notification permission is a typed Flux capability: API 33+ grant state can be queried/requested directly, older Android versions require no runtime prompt, and generated manifests include `POST_NOTIFICATIONS` only for reachable notification code.
  - [x] General Android permissions are declarative and typed without Gradle: `[android].permissions = ["android.permission.CAMERA", ...]` is parsed, validated, deterministically de-duplicated, and emitted as `<uses-permission>` entries; `android.allowed(permission)` and `android.ask(permission)` lower directly to compiler-owned framework JNI and appear in static checking plus LSP completion/signature help.
- [x] Background services/tasks where allowed: typed `android.schedule(jobId, delayMs)` registers a one-shot native Android `JobScheduler` job through compiler-generated `JobService`/DEX/manifest glue, `android.cancelJob(jobId)` cancels it, and application `onBackgroundJob: fn(i64) -> void` receives execution directly in compiled Flux. Invalid negative/out-of-range job IDs and negative delays fail safely, unreachable scheduling/cancellation helpers and service glue tree-shake, and application source writes no Java/Kotlin/JNI bridge, method channel, serialization bus, or plugin registry. AndroidX WorkManager remains separately tracked above for richer constraints/chaining/guaranteed-work semantics.
- [x] Camera: typed `android.camera(callback)` launches Android's native `IMAGE_CAPTURE` flow through compiler-owned Activity/JNI glue, persists successful full-resolution JPEG output in `MediaStore`, and lends the captured content URI only for the exact `fn(str) -> void` callback scope. Cancellation/launch failure removes provisional media entries, concurrent capture attempts are rejected while one result is pending, unreachable support tree-shakes completely, and application source needs no Kotlin/Java/JNI bridge, method channel, serialization bus, or plugin registry.
- [x] Microphone: typed `android.record(path)` / `android.stopRecord()` lower through compiler-owned direct JNI to Android `MediaRecorder`, use AAC-in-MP4 file capture, return explicit success/failure, infer `RECORD_AUDIO` only when reachable recording code exists, tree-shake when unused, and require no application-authored Kotlin/Java/JNI bridge or plugin channel.
- [x] Media playback: typed `android.play(source)`, `android.pause()`, `android.resume()`, and `android.stop()` lower directly through compiler-owned JNI to Android `MediaPlayer`/`Uri`, keep at most one compiler-owned live player, explicitly release replaced/stopped players, return success/failure without exceptions crossing into Flux, tree-shake completely when unreachable, and require no application-authored Kotlin/Java/JNI bridge, method channel, serialization bus, or plugin registry.
- [x] Secure storage: typed `android.store`, `android.load`, and `android.erase` lower through compiler-owned JNI into a generated Android-only storage helper using an app-scoped Android Keystore AES-GCM key plus private encrypted `SharedPreferences` ciphertext. Reads lend decrypted text only for callback scope, API 23 is enforced by binding availability, generated JVM glue/Dex tree-shakes when unreachable, and applications write no platform bridge code.
- [x] Files/media picker: typed `android.file`, `android.media`, and `android.folder` launch Android's native Storage Access Framework directly from ordinary Flux code and lend the selected content URI to an exact `fn(str) -> void` callback. Compiler-generated `FluxActivity`/JNI owns request/result routing and persistable URI grants, cancellation clears pending callbacks without inventing a nullable source value, unreachable picker support tree-shakes from both native and generated-Java glue, and application source needs no Activity/Kotlin/Java/JNI bridge.
- [x] Android keyboard/IME: the current native Android surface covers platform-owned text composition, keyboard-type selection, submit policy, compiler-managed caret/selection/composition preservation across required rebuilds, direct show/hide IME control, focus restoration and scoped traversal, focused selection/caret mutation, and typed IME action configuration. The integrated Android codegen regression verifies these helpers lower through direct platform/JNI calls, reject non-Android reachability, tree-shake when unused, and introduce no method-channel/plugin bridge.
  - [x] Bootstrap native text input supports compiler-managed autofocus, password input type, maximum length, live `onChange: fn(str)`, native multiline editing, compile-time `submitOnEnter` policy, and IME Done/Enter `onSubmit: fn(str)` callbacks without application Java/Kotlin code.
  - [x] Portable compile-time `TextInput.keyboardType` selects `text`, `email`, `number`, `decimal`, `phone`, or `url`; Android lowers to the corresponding native `InputType` flags and Linux lowers to the corresponding GTK input purpose, with unsupported values rejected at compile time.
  - [x] Compiler-owned Android text caching preserves caret/selection plus active IME composing ranges across the full native-view rebuilds still required for configuration/layout recreation; ordinary functional state transitions now avoid that rebuild path entirely.
  - [x] `android.showKeyboard()` / `android.hideKeyboard()` directly control the soft keyboard for the currently focused native view through compiler-owned `InputMethodManager` JNI and tree-shake away when unused.
  - [x] Android full-view rebuilds required by configuration/layout recreation preserve the currently focused stable element ID and restore focus onto its replacement without re-firing Flux focus transitions during compiler-driven replacement; ordinary state refresh preserves the existing focused view directly.
  - [x] Programmatic focus movement for the current native control through typed `android.next()`, `android.prior()`, and `android.blur()` calls; Android lowers these through compiler-owned `getCurrentFocus`/`focusSearch`/`requestFocus`/`clearFocus` JNI, tree-shakes the helper when unused, exposes completion/signature help, and was verified on a physical Samsung by moving focus first → second → first and clearing it.
  - [x] Explicit traversal-edge targeting through typed `android.first()` / `android.last()`: compiler-owned JNI uses Android `FocusFinder` against the Activity content root, requests the first forward/last backward focus target, tree-shakes the helper when unused, and exposes both calls through static checking plus LSP completion/signature help.
  - [x] Focused `TextInput` selection/caret APIs: `android.start()` / `end()` return the current native selection or `-1` when unavailable; `android.caret(position)` / `select(start, end)` return `bool` and reject missing focus or invalid/out-of-range positions rather than clamping or throwing through JNI. The helper verifies an actual `EditText`, tree-shakes when unused, appears in LSP completion/signature help, and was verified on physical Samsung hardware by inserting at caret 2 (`abcd` → `abXcd`) and replacing selection 1..3 (`abXcd` → `aYcd`).
  - [x] Focused Android IME action configuration through typed `android.ime(action)`: `none`, `go`, `search`, `send`, `next`, `done`, and `previous` map directly to native `EditText.setImeOptions` through compiler-owned JNI, return `false` for unsupported actions or missing/non-text focus, participate in LSP completion/signature help, and tree-shake completely when unreachable; no application Kotlin/Java bridge is required.
  - [x] Multiline submit policy: Android `TextInput.multiline` uses native multi-line input flags; `submitOnEnter` defaults to `false` for multiline editors and `true` for single-line fields, while an explicit `true` consumes Enter/IME Done through the typed `onSubmit` callback. Numeric/decimal/phone keyboard modes and password input reject multiline combinations that do not have coherent native text semantics. Linux now follows the same default Enter policy through native `GtkTextView`/`GtkTextBuffer` lowering; multiline max-length uses the native buffer insertion guard, multiline placeholder uses a native non-interactive overlay, and password remains explicitly rejected rather than silently degrading to a single-line entry.
  - [x] Configurable traversal wrapping: `android.next()` / `prior()` keep non-wrapping native traversal by default, while an optional typed `wrap: bool` retries from the opposite native focus edge only when ordinary `focusSearch` cannot move focus. The no-argument path retains its smaller tree-shaken runtime and does not pull in `FocusFinder` edge traversal unless wrapping or explicit first/last focus is reachable.
  - [x] Portable focus scopes and broader configurable traversal policy: compile-time `focusScope: i64` groups native controls without introducing a focus-manager object, while typed `focus.nextIn(scope, wrap)`, `focus.priorIn(scope, wrap)`, `focus.firstIn(scope)`, and `focus.lastIn(scope)` traverse only that scope. Linux walks the real GTK widget tree, Android enumerates native platform focusables through compiler-owned direct JNI/View APIs, and Windows scans the live child-HWND tab order plus compiler-owned scope properties before calling native `SetFocus`; application code writes no platform bridge and unused scoped traversal helpers tree-shake with ordinary reachability.
- [x] Platform FFI/bindings to Kotlin/Java/NDK where needed: ordinary Android framework access remains compiler-owned through the centralized `android.*` binding table plus generated JNI/JVM glue, while the advanced native escape hatch now permits the same narrow ABI-safe typed `extern c` imports on Android and feeds validated `[native].libraries` / `[native].search_paths` directly into the selected ABI's NDK Clang link. No application-authored Java/Kotlin/JNI bridge, method channel, serialization bus, or plugin registry is required.
- [x] Verify generated Android glue is tree-shakeable and zero/near-zero overhead beyond boundaries Android itself requires; no mandatory Flutter-style channel serialization or plugin registry.
  - [x] Android runtime helpers are emitted from reachable generated-call usage rather than linked as a permanent platform runtime. Compiler tests prove unreachable notification channel/post/action/cancel code omits `Notification.Builder`, `PendingIntent`, notification helpers, and permission machinery entirely; platform calls cross JNI directly with no serialization bus, plugin registry, or user-written bridge.
- [x] Play Store-ready release pipeline for compiler-owned upload artifacts: `flux publish android <package>` performs a non-interactive release/all-ABI AAB build, refuses development-key fallback, requires explicit version metadata plus configured upload-key credentials, enforces the current API 36+ phone/tablet submission target, and validates the final signed bundle with both `bundletool validate` and standard `jarsigner` signature verification. Play Console account/listing/policy/rollout actions remain external rather than hidden compiler side effects.
  - [x] Publishing version metadata is configurable without Gradle: `[package].version` becomes Android `versionName`, `[android].version_code` becomes `versionCode`, defaults to 1, and rejects values outside Google Play's accepted 1..=2100000000 range. The default targetSdk is API 36 for current phone/tablet Play submissions, and signed all-ABI AAB generation remains directly consumable by Play App Signing.
- [x] First real production-style Flux Android app built and tested on physical hardware: the polished `examples/mobile_showcase` application compiles from Flux source into the generated native Android activity/APK and has been repeatedly installed/launched successfully on a physical Samsung SM-S931B, including the semantic typography, native styling, scrolling, toggle state, and primary-action surface used by the README showcase.

# Milestone 16 — Desktop targets

## Linux

  - [x] Linux image source lowering bounds every borrowed source to 65536 bytes before asset inspection or native path construction, matching the Windows backend’s bounded source contract.

- [x] Native Linux window/render/input backend: the current GTK4 backend owns the real Linux application window, native flat-grid rendering, pointer/keyboard/text input, focus, and direct callback dispatch without a browser/widget-emulation runtime. A dedicated compiler regression now exercises window + Text + TextInput + Button lowering together and rejects Android/web runtime leakage.
  - [x] Bootstrap GTK4 backend opens a real native Linux window, lowers Flux flat grid/Text/Button to native controls, and dispatches native button clicks into Flux callbacks.
- [x] Wayland-first support with appropriate fallback where practical.
  - [x] Bootstrap GUI was built and launched end-to-end on a real `wayland-0` compositor (`GDK_BACKEND=wayland`); GTK4 supplies the native Wayland integration while the eventual lower-level backend remains open.
  - [x] Linux GUI startup now prefers GDK's Wayland backend before X11/other available backends when the user has not selected a backend explicitly, while preserving `GDK_BACKEND` as the authoritative opt-in override for debugging, compatibility, and non-Wayland sessions.
- [x] Native Linux menus/tray/clipboard/file dialogs: `menu.show(title, items, callback)` lowers a non-empty compile-time menu directly to a GTK4 `GtkPopoverMenuBar` with indexed `GSimpleAction` dispatch, while `tray.show(title, iconName, callback)` registers a compiler-owned freedesktop StatusNotifierItem on the desktop session bus and routes native activation directly into Flux. Existing portable clipboard and native file-dialog APIs remain part of the same desktop surface; all helpers are application-only, Linux-target-checked, tree-shaken when unreachable, and require no user-written GTK/D-Bus bridge or plugin registry.
  - [x] Clipboard for the current native application backends: canonical `clipboard.write(text)` writes native plain text on Linux/Android, while `clipboard.read(callback)` safely lends clipboard text only for the duration of an exact `fn(str) -> void` callback instead of returning ownership-ambiguous GUI storage. The older `setText`/`readText` spellings remain source-compatible only. Linux uses GDK's asynchronous text-read API, Android reads/coerces the primary `ClipData` item through compiler-owned JNI, LSP completion/signature help expose the canonical operations, unreachable helpers tree-shake, and headless use is rejected.
  - [x] Native Linux file dialogs for the current desktop backend: canonical `fileDialog.open`, `fileDialog.save`, and `fileDialog.folder` accept exact `fn(str) -> void` callbacks and lower to `GtkFileChooserNative`, lending the selected local path only for callback scope before compiler-owned native storage is released. Legacy longer spellings remain source-compatible only. The helpers attach to the active GTK application window, tree-shake when unreachable, reject headless use, and fail Android target lowering explicitly until a native Android picker implementation exists; applications write no GTK bridge code.
- [ ] Multi-window.
- [x] Packaging formats/integration guidance for the current Linux target: `flux package` produces either the existing host-labelled directory bundle or a directly distributable `--format tar.gz` archive with normalized ordering/timestamps/ownership metadata; same-host identical archives are byte-reproducibility tested, extracted archives execute successfully, and distro-specific deb/rpm/Arch/AppImage/Flatpak integration remains documented as an explicit downstream layer rather than hidden compiler behavior.

## Windows

- [ ] Native Windows window/render/input backend. Bootstrap Windows-target lowering now emits a real Win32 top-level window plus native `STATIC`, `BUTTON`, and `EDIT` controls for Text/Button/TextInput, Toggle/Radio, and the current semantic text elements from the portable flat grid. Button/toggle/radio activation, passive native `onTap`, and text-change dispatch reach typed Flux callbacks directly; tappable static/semantic controls opt into native `SS_NOTIFY`/`WM_COMMAND` dispatch rather than a synthetic event bridge. Toggle/radio state transitions refresh in place, and controlled TextInput state now owns incoming text safely, refreshes from the canonical `text` property, supports native Enter submission, placeholder cues, read-only/password/max-length options, and recomputes derived values without rebuilding controls. State/derived text plus common visibility/enabled and checked/selected values refresh existing controls in place. The Win32 surface is process-DPI-aware: portable logical window metrics convert to/from physical client pixels, grid padding/gaps/minimums scale at the native boundary, `WM_SIZE` preserves logical `windowWidth`/`windowHeight`, and `WM_DPICHANGED` adopts the platform-suggested bounds before relayout/refresh. Native Text now applies semantic `variant` defaults plus compile-time size/bold/italic/underline/strikethrough through compiler-owned Win32 fonts rebuilt for per-monitor DPI changes. Portable `maxWidthChars` now constrains native Text width using the selected Win32 font metrics, with semantic-variant defaults and state/derived width changes relaid out in place. `maxLines` now caps native Text height from the same selected-font metrics, validates static/dynamic line limits, and relayouts existing HWNDs in place. `accessibilityLabel` / `accessibilityDescription` refresh through compiler-owned direct HWND accessibility annotation and compile-time `accessibilityRole` maps to native MSAA roles without exposing COM/Win32 objects to Flux source. State-driven `accessibilityHidden` now uses native MSAA state annotation with default-state restoration when unhidden, `accessibilityValue` and primary `accessibilityActionLabel` map directly to native value/default-action properties, and dynamic `focusable` updates the HWND tab-stop style in place. Application `resizable: false` now removes the native resize frame/maximize affordance while preserving ordinary resizable Win32 windows by default. Application `id` now also becomes the process AppUserModelID through dynamically resolved Shell32 integration, giving packaged and unpackaged windows stable native taskbar/shell grouping without exposing a Windows API or adding a hard shell-library link. The root window, built-in system controls, and common tooltips now stay on Unicode Win32 registration/creation/message paths: Flux UTF-8 titles, labels, tooltip text, and TextInput edits cross the native boundary through explicit UTF-16 conversion, focused TextInput selection uses the Unicode EDIT contract, and core UI behavior no longer depends on the active ANSI code page. Explicit application `layoutDirection: "rtl"` now mirrors the native top-level Win32 layout through `WS_EX_LAYOUTRTL`, while `ltr`/`system` preserve the ordinary Windows layout, so child controls inherit native RTL mirroring without a framework layer. Native keyboard/focus parity now stays on the direct Win32 path: compile-time `autofocus` calls `SetFocus`, `onKey` observes focused-control key messages with the portable key vocabulary, `onFocus` / `onBlur` use compiler-owned child-window procedures, `IsDialogMessage` supplies native Tab traversal, and passive tappable semantic controls join the native tab order and route Enter/Space through the same compiled `onTap` action unless explicit `onKey` takes precedence. Broader styling and heading-depth parity remain before this backend is complete; native Windows E2E validation now runs on a Windows CI host through the ordinary host-default `fluxc build` path and proves the generated executable reaches `onStart` before its message loop. The minimal Win32 application path now always emits its root `HWND` storage independently of optional Windows capabilities, with regression coverage that pins declaration-before-use and a Unicode cursor resource. Host-native Windows linking now recognizes the Unicode `CreateWindowExW` path when selecting `user32`, preventing the Windows smoke executable from compiling successfully and then failing at link time after the Unicode migration. Windows release builds now select LLVM `lld` explicitly when Clang LTO is enabled, so release-mode MSIX packaging uses the same optimized native path without depending on an incompatible default MSVC linker; the MSIX CI smoke also stops immediately when package construction fails.
  - [x] Windows image source lowering bounds every borrowed source to 65536 bytes before path inspection, duplication, or asset-path construction, preventing native `strstr`/`strlen` scans from reading beyond the Flux string contract.
# Verified Windows font-family follow-up: `Text.fontFamily` now lowers through compiler-owned UTF-8/UTF-16 conversion into each native Win32 `HFONT`, preserves semantic size/emphasis, recreates fonts during DPI refresh, frees replaced fonts and conversion buffers, and rejects empty or invalid compile-time values. Generated-Win32 regression coverage verifies the native face conversion and failure contract; broader styling, heading-depth parity, remaining controls, and native Windows E2E validation remain open.
- [x] Windows native portable text drag/drop parity: `dragText` begins a native OLE copy drag only after the platform drag threshold, publishes bounded UTF-8 payloads as `CF_UNICODETEXT`, and `onDrop` registers compiler-owned `IDropTarget` instances that validate/copy text back to UTF-8 before invoking the existing typed `fn(str) -> void` callback and refreshing in place. Drop targets are revoked before HWND destruction, OLE apartment lifetime is paired with the app loop, `<ole2.h>` / `ole32` are emitted only when reachable, and the portable drag/drop regression plus the Windows generated-C smoke fixture cover the lowering without exposing COM objects to Flux source.
- [x] Windows native Button emphasis/typography parity: `Button.primary` maps directly to `BS_DEFPUSHBUTTON`/`BS_PUSHBUTTON` and may refresh from view state without replacing the HWND; `Button.size` owns a compiler-managed DPI-aware `HFONT`, validates static and runtime sizes consistently with the other native backends, rebuilds only when size or monitor DPI changes, and releases the font at shutdown. No button/controller object is exposed to Flux source.
- [x] Windows native text-input scope parity: `TextInput.keyboardType` maps `text`, `email`, `number`, `decimal`, `phone`, and `url` to the corresponding Windows Text Services Framework input scopes through compiler-owned `SetInputScope` dispatch. Static values are validated during lowering, state/derived values refresh the existing EDIT HWND in place, the optional `Msctf.dll` entry point is resolved without a hard link dependency, and scopes are cleared before normal window destruction.
- [x] Windows dynamic Text typography parity: `fontFamily`, `size`, `bold`, `italic`, `underline`, and `strikethrough` now refresh compiler-owned Win32 `HFONT` values in place from state/derived expressions. Font replacement is cached by family/style/size/DPI, applies before text-metric layout refresh, validates dynamic values at the native boundary, preserves compile-time diagnostics for invalid static values, and releases both GDI fonts and owned family caches at shutdown.
- [x] Win32/modern Windows integration as appropriate: generic native builds select Windows codegen for Windows Clang triples, package `[platform.windows]` module substitution uses the same stable logical imports as Linux/Android, portable desktop capabilities lower directly to compiler-owned Win32 APIs, and typed `windows.processId`, `windows.uptimeMillis`, `windows.messageBox`, `windows.open`, `windows.beep`, `windows.screenWidth`, and `windows.screenHeight` bind directly to `MessageBoxW`, `ShellExecuteW`, `Beep`, and `GetSystemMetrics`. Target-gated lowering and reachable-feature system-library selection keep the path native-first without method channels, plugin registries, or user-written Win32 bridges; distribution remains tracked separately by MSIX/store packaging.
- [x] Menus/tray/clipboard/file dialogs: the existing portable `menu.show`, `tray.show`, `clipboard.write`/`clipboard.read`, and `fileDialog.open`/`save`/`folder` APIs now lower directly to compiler-owned Win32 menu, notification-area, Unicode clipboard, and native common/folder dialog APIs. Reachable features select only their required Windows system libraries, callbacks receive temporary compiler-owned UTF-8 values without exposing Win32 handles or user-written bridge code, and the same source APIs remain native on Linux while Android rejects desktop-only surfaces explicitly.
- [x] Windows bitmap `Image` sources load through compiler-owned Win32 bitmap controls with refresh-time handle cleanup; `asset://` sources resolve safely beneath the staged executable-adjacent `assets/` directory with traversal rejection. The portable `Image.fit` modes now resize native bitmaps against the live control bounds for fill, contain, cover, and scaleDown, including state-driven refreshes.
- [x] Windows `Image.alt` now maps to the native MSAA accessible name and refreshes from state/derived values in place; an explicit `accessibilityLabel` remains authoritative, matching the portable accessibility contract without exposing Win32 objects to Flux source.
- [x] Windows common tooltips now use the native common-controls tooltip window for the existing portable `tooltip: str` property. Each HWND is registered directly with the compiler-owned tooltip manager, state/derived tooltip text refreshes in place through bounded copied storage so UI-state replacement cannot leave a dangling native pointer, and unreachable tooltip support adds no common-controls runtime/link dependency.
- [x] Windows native controls now honor the portable `minWidth` / `minHeight` / `maxWidth` / `maxHeight` constraints directly in the compiler-owned Win32 grid layout. Static constraints fail lowering when invalid, state/derived constraints are range/relationship-checked at runtime, DPI scaling is applied only at the native boundary, and dynamic constraint changes relayout the existing HWNDs in place without wrapper controls or a layout-object runtime.
- [x] Windows common margins now share the portable `margin` plus per-edge override contract. Static and state/derived margin values are validated as non-negative logical dimensions, start/top offsets and end/bottom insets are applied directly during DPI-aware HWND layout, and dynamic margin changes relayout the existing controls without rebuilding them.
- [x] Windows `TextInput.backgroundColor` now reaches native EDIT controls through `WM_CTLCOLOREDIT`, reusing the existing static/dynamic semantic color storage and refresh path rather than silently generating an unused brush.
- [x] Windows `TextInput.validationMessage` now participates in native feedback: non-empty validation text takes temporary precedence over the ordinary tooltip, refreshes through compiler-owned bounded tooltip storage, and supplies the MSAA description when no explicit `accessibilityDescription` is present. Clearing validation text restores the ordinary tooltip without rebuilding the native edit control.
- [x] Windows `TextInput.validationState` now maps `normal` / `error` / `success` / `warning` onto native EDIT semantic foreground feedback through `WM_CTLCOLOREDIT`; state/derived changes repaint the existing HWND in place, `normal` restores the system text color, and explicit TextInput background styling remains authoritative without a wrapper/controller object.
- [x] Windows accessibility traversal order parity: compile-time `accessibilityOrder` now reorders the generated child HWND sequence through `SetWindowPos` after native controls are constructed, preserving geometry and activation while aligning native child/dialog traversal with the portable order; generated Win32 regression coverage verifies the sorted chain without exposing a Windows accessibility object to Flux source.
- [x] Windows accessibility action-label fallback parity: `accessibilityLongPressLabel` and named `accessibilityActions` labels are preserved through compiler-owned MSAA descriptions when no explicit accessibility description or TextInput validation description takes precedence, while `accessibilityActionLabel` continues to map to the native default-action property; this preserves discoverable semantics without pretending Win32 exposes Android-style arbitrary custom accessibility actions.
- [x] Windows native hover/leave parity: common `onHover` / `onLeave` callbacks and functional state transitions now subclass the existing child HWND directly, arm `TrackMouseEvent(TME_LEAVE)` on the first native mouse move, dispatch enter once and leave on `WM_MOUSELEAVE`, and compose with existing input/focus subclass chains without a source-level event/controller object.
- [x] Windows native double-tap parity: `onDoubleTap` callbacks and functional state transitions now recognize two primary-button releases using the OS `GetDoubleClickTime` interval and `SM_CXDOUBLECLK`/`SM_CYDOUBLECLK` movement bounds on the existing child HWND; native single-click handling runs first and remains intact, and the recognizer composes through the same subclass chain without a source-level gesture object.
- [x] Windows native long-press parity: `onLongPress` callbacks and functional state transitions now arm a compiler-owned Win32 timer using the OS click timing, cancel when primary-button motion exceeds the native `SM_CXDRAG`/`SM_CYDRAG` thresholds or the press ends, and dispatch once while the primary button remains held. A recognized long press consumes the matching native click notification so Button/onTap behavior matches the existing Android long-click contract, and the recognizer composes through the existing child-HWND subclass chain without a source-level gesture object.
- [x] Windows native drag-offset parity: `onDrag` now captures the existing child HWND across a primary-button drag, reports cumulative X/Y offsets from the press origin in Flux logical units through DPI-aware signed unscaling, releases capture on button-up/capture loss, and dispatches the typed callback directly before refreshing existing state. Native child behavior continues through the subclass chain and no source-level pointer/controller object is exposed.
- [x] Windows native swipe parity: `onSwipe` tracks a primary-button pointer sequence on the existing child HWND, uses native `SM_CXDRAG`/`SM_CYDRAG` motion thresholds plus `GetDoubleClickTime` to derive a platform-sensitive fling gate, and reports DPI-unscaled logical X/Y velocity in pixels per second to the typed `fn(i64, i64) -> void` callback. Pointer capture keeps the release observable outside the control, crossing the native drag threshold suppresses the matching click/tap notification even when a slow drag does not qualify as a swipe, and no source-level gesture object is introduced.
- [x] Windows native pinch-scale parity: `onScale` configures the existing child HWND for native `GID_ZOOM` delivery, reads two-point distance from `GESTUREINFO.ullArguments` across `GF_BEGIN`/`GF_END`, and reports the same rounded relative scale percentage used by Android/GTK to the typed `fn(i64) -> void` callback. Handled gesture-info handles are closed immediately, the recognizer composes through the child-HWND subclass chain, and no source-level gesture/controller object is introduced.
- [x] Windows portable focus-navigation parity: ordinary and scoped `focus.next` / `previous` / `first` / `last` / `clear` helpers now operate over the live child-HWND tab order, honor visibility/enabled/tab-stop state, use compiler-owned `SetPropA` metadata for compile-time `focusScope`, preserve wrap/edge behavior, and move focus only through native `SetFocus` without a focus-manager object.
- [x] Windows portable TextInput selection parity: `textInput.selectionStart` / `selectionEnd` / `setCaret` / `setSelection` now resolve the focused native EDIT HWND, read selection/caret positions through `EM_GETSEL`, validate bounds against the live control text, and mutate selection through `EM_SETSEL`; helpers remain reachability-driven and no text-editor wrapper object is introduced.
- [x] Windows Flux color styling validates hex/semantic colors and lowers Text/background brushes through native `WM_CTLCOLORSTATIC`/`WM_CTLCOLORBTN` handling with compiler-owned brush cleanup; runtime state/derived colors replace brushes and invalidate only the existing controls in place, while the remaining broader styling surface stays pending.
- [x] Windows semantic presentation-status parity: portable compile-time `status` values validate against `normal` / `loading` / `empty` / `error`; loading lowers to native child-window alpha at the same 68% presentation level used by Linux/Android, while empty/error text-capable controls use the shared `textMuted` / `danger` semantic colors through the existing `WM_CTLCOLOR*` path. No presentation wrapper or framework object is introduced.
- [x] Windows selectable Text bootstrap: compile-time `Text.selectable: true` lowers to a borderless native read-only multiline EDIT with keyboard focus and normal Windows selection/copy behavior; explicit false/omitted Text remains a STATIC label. State-driven selectability, dynamic alignment on selectable text, and the selectable+onTap composition fail Windows lowering explicitly instead of silently losing semantics, pending a mutation-safe native interaction path.
- [x] Windows Text overflow honesty/native end truncation: compile-time `wrap: true` and `wrapMode: "word"` map to the native STATIC/EDIT wrapping behavior, while `ellipsize: "end"` adds Win32 `SS_ENDELLIPSIS` for ordinary Text. Left/fill no-wrap lowers natively through `SS_LEFTNOWORDWRAP` for ordinary Text and multiline EDIT plus `ES_AUTOHSCROLL` for selectable Text; centered/right ordinary no-wrap is also native when end ellipsis is present because `SS_ENDELLIPSIS` forces single-line rendering while preserving alignment. Centered/right clipped no-wrap without ellipsis remains an explicit target diagnostic, and state-driven alignment is rejected for clipped no-wrap so a refresh cannot silently re-enable wrapping. Unsupported char/wordChar wrapping, start/middle ellipsis, richText, letter spacing, and custom line height continue to fail Windows target lowering explicitly instead of being accepted and silently ignored; those richer modes remain part of the broader Windows text-rendering work.
- [x] Windows native translation parity: common `translateX` / `translateY` values, including state/derived expressions, now feed the existing DPI-aware child-HWND layout and move controls in place during refresh. Static and runtime values enforce the portable 32-bit transform bound, negative offsets scale correctly, and coordinate addition saturates safely before `MoveWindow`.
- [x] Windows native radius styling: common `radius` values keep the direct `CreateRoundRectRgn` fast path, while `radiusTopLeft` / `radiusTopRight` / `radiusBottomRight` / `radiusBottomLeft` compose compiler-owned Win32 regions for asymmetric corners. Uniform values remain the fallback for unspecified corners, static and state/derived radii are range-checked, DPI scaling/clamping happens at the native boundary, zero removes clipping, and layout refresh reapplies regions in place without wrapper controls.
- [x] Windows native border styling: `borderColor`, `borderWidth`, and `borderStyle: "solid"` / `"none"` paint through compiler-owned child HWND subclasses after native control rendering, refresh from state/derived values in place, and scale widths at the current DPI. `borderTop` / `borderEnd` / `borderBottom` / `borderStart` color and width overrides now share that painter, inherit the uniform value when omitted, refresh explicit dynamic edges independently, clip to the active uniform/asymmetric radius region, and swap logical start/end at the Win32 RTL boundary. Equal edges retain the native `FrameRgn` fast path; dashed/dotted/double styles remain explicit target diagnostics instead of being silently discarded.
- [x] Windows styling capability honesty: portable element alignment/padding, clipping overrides, shadows, rotation/scale/skew/origin transforms, transition styling/layout animation, and gesture-driven `dragTranslate` / `pinchScale` are no longer accepted and silently dropped by the Win32 target. Until those semantics have faithful native lowering they fail target code generation at the exact property span; supported translation, dimensions/margins, colors, radii, and border styling continue to lower normally.
- [x] Generated Windows GUI C is syntax-validated against available Wine Win32 headers (`-fshort-wchar`) and linked with `winegcc` when that toolchain is present. A dedicated `windows-latest` compiler-self-test job now exercises the native host path end to end: target inference selects Windows without requiring `--target`, `fluxc build` invokes host Clang and Win32 system libraries, the produced `.exe` launches, and an `onStart` callback exits with a sentinel code before the message loop.
- [ ] Multi-window.
- [x] MSIX/store packaging.
  - [x] MSIX bundle generation is available through `flux package --format msix --target <windows-triple>`: the compiler stages the native executable/assets, writes a versioned full-trust Appx manifest and placeholder tile assets, and emits a deterministic metadata-stripped ZIP container. The ZIP entries are stored without DEFLATE compression so the compiler-generated block map can correctly omit per-block stored-size metadata while still matching the MSIX block-map schema. A signed release may supply `--certificate <publisher.pfx> --publisher <identity>`; signing uses Windows `signtool` with SHA-256 and replaces the output only after success. Publisher identities are validated as exact non-empty `CN=...` certificate-subject-shaped values before manifest emission; certificate identity provisioning and Microsoft Store submission are intentionally external release-owner operations.

# Milestone 18 — Web target

Web is a supported target, not the implementation substrate for native apps. The default web model should use the browser platform directly: semantic DOM, CSS, browser APIs, and compiler-generated JavaScript where needed. WASM is an optional backend/optimization choice for suitable application code, not Flux's web runtime.

- [x] Bootstrap browser target: `flux build web <file.flux|package-dir|flux.toml> [-o directory]` type-checks an ordinary Flux `app`/root view and emits a directly loadable self-contained `index.html` without a VM, browser framework, canvas widget-emulation layer, or user-authored JavaScript bridge. The current bootstrap backend covers declarative application UI, view state/derived expressions, and direct state-transition events; broader general application-code lowering remains explicitly tracked by the optional WASM/application-code item rather than hiding a second source/runtime model.
- [x] Optional/automatic WASM lowering for suitable application code: ordinary web apps continue to default to direct DOM/CSS/browser-API integration plus compiler-generated JavaScript, while `--wasm auto` may lower the current pure `i64` application-function slice into an embedded WebAssembly module. `--wasm never` deterministically keeps direct JavaScript lowering and `--wasm always` requires every reachable application function to fit the supported WASM slice or fails explicitly. WASM remains an optional compiler backend inside the self-contained web artifact, not a mandatory Flux web runtime, framework layer, UI renderer, plugin channel, or user-authored JavaScript bridge.
- [x] DOM/browser-native integration for the current declarative UI surface: Flux built-in elements lower to native semantic DOM controls/containers (`button`, `input`, `img`, `label`+checkbox/radio, `nav`, `figure`, `article`, `header`, `section`, and text nodes) positioned by the compiler-owned grid. Typed view-state transitions attach direct DOM event listeners and refresh the existing nodes in place, with no method-channel/plugin registry/serialized bridge or framework virtual-widget tree.
- [x] Browser routing/history for the current declarative web backend: typed `browser.path()`, `browser.push(path)`, `browser.replace(path)`, `browser.back()`, and `browser.forward()` lower directly to the browser Location/History APIs. Push/replace and native `popstate` traversal refresh the existing Flux DOM in place, while ordinary UI event callbacks can invoke these operations without a framework router, serialized channel, or user-authored JavaScript bridge.
- [x] Browser storage for the current declarative web backend: typed `browser.store(key, value)`, `browser.load(key, fallback)`, and `browser.erase(key)` lower directly to `localStorage`; reads provide an explicit fallback for missing/unavailable storage, and UI event callbacks call the browser primitive directly without exposing storage objects, a plugin registry, or application-authored JavaScript glue.
- [x] Browser accessibility/semantics for the current web backend: semantic native controls are preserved and Flux `accessibilityLabel`, `accessibilityDescription`, `accessibilityHidden`, and `accessibilityRole` lower to browser `aria-*`/role state on the real DOM nodes. Role spellings are normalized to valid web roles while ordinary native button/input/image semantics remain authoritative instead of creating a parallel accessibility tree.
- [x] Responsive web layout for the current web backend: Flux grid tracks lower directly to CSS Grid (`px`/`fr`/`auto`) with typed gap/padding/scroll behavior, while `windowWidth`, `windowHeight`, orientation, compact/medium/expanded breakpoints, and `displayScale` read browser viewport/device state and re-run in-place UI refresh on native resize events. The generated viewport metadata and full-width root therefore preserve the same portable responsive-expression contract without a browser framework runtime.
- [x] Development server for the bootstrap web target: `flux run web <file.flux|package-dir|flux.toml> [--host address] [--port number]` serves the compiler-emitted DOM bundle directly over a small compiler-owned HTTP server, defaults to loopback-only `127.0.0.1:4173`, supports explicit host/port overrides, and keeps browser development transport separate from production artifacts.
- [x] Automatic save-triggered refresh for the bootstrap web target: the development server fingerprints package `flux.toml`/`.flux` sources, recompiles after a source-tree change is observed, and exposes a compiler-owned version endpoint polled by the served page so successful saves reload the browser automatically. Invalid intermediate saves report diagnostics while preserving the last successful page until the next source change; production `flux build web` output contains no reload endpoint/runtime.
- [x] Production bundling/tree shaking for the current declarative web backend: `flux build web` emits one self-contained `index.html` containing only the selected application/root view, that view's state/derived expressions and direct DOM event logic, omitting unused sibling views and all development-server reload code. Regression coverage verifies both the single-file production bundle and that unused view content is absent; finer state/expression pruning can extend the same direct emitter as broader application-code lowering lands.

# Milestone 19 — Platform interoperability and plugin ecosystem

- [x] Stable C FFI for the versioned package export boundary: manifest-backed packages expose ABI v2 through generated C11/C++-compatible headers, package/module-qualified linker symbols, documented scalar/borrowed-string/primitive-optional/recursive `Copy` aggregate layouts, explicit enum tags, multi-return structs, and allocation-free `Copy` callbacks. Regression coverage links and executes a native C consumer across representative supported value categories. Unsupported ownership-sensitive values remain rejected rather than receiving an unstable layout, while native imports/link metadata and broader foreign ownership remain separately tracked below.
